Changelog

What changed on the street, and when. Every Stimpunks site publishes one of these.

Subscribe to this page — the feed is generated from it, so the two cannot disagree.

2026-10-21 — Mentions in Tasks and the office

  • An @mention in Tasks is drawn as the person’s name and opens their profile, in descriptions and comments, and in the office’s comments, as it already was on the CB and in the forums. Yours is marked.
  • Type @ and a name in a task’s description or any comment there or in the office, and pick from the list of the directors and administrators.

2026-10-21 — Links and Linked from, after Notion’s

  • Type [[ and part of a name in a message on the CB, a forum post, a task or a comment, or a record in the office, and pick a forum topic, or for our directors and administrators a task, a grant request, a case or a contact, to link it. It is drawn as what it is and its name.
  • A link never carries a name. The name is shown only to the people who could open it; everybody else sees that it is one they can’t open.
  • Linked from, on a task, under a topic and on an office record, lists what links to it, of what you could open yourself. Chats can hold links and are never in it, because the CB keeps them for 48 hours. Privacy says what it keeps.

2026-10-21 — Previews in the lists, and the forums one press away

  • Rest the pointer on a task’s name in Tasks’ list, or on a grant request’s or a case’s in the office, and a preview shows beside it, Norah’s ask: what happened to it last, the first parts of its description and its latest comments. Landing on the name with the keyboard shows it too, Esc puts it away, and the pointer can move onto it to read it.
  • The forums’ bar has Tasks and Office for our directors and administrators, and Tasks’ bar and the office’s have Forums, so each is a press from the others.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: Group by

  • Tasks’ list can be grouped, after Notion’s By assignee: Group by Whose puts each task under every person on it, assigned or also on it, so each person’s heading has all of theirs. Tags, Assigned to, Status and Priority group it too, a heading folds its group away, and a saved filter keeps the grouping.
  • A filter saved in the feed is kept now. Until today the server refused it.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: coloured statuses, and Whose

  • A task’s status is a coloured pill now, after Notion’s, in the list, on the board, in the feed and on the task: Not Started gray, Started the light blue Notion gives In progress, Completed green, Canceled brown and Deferred yellow, each with its name on it.
  • Tasks’ list has a Whose column: who it is assigned to and who else is on it, together, so you can sort by everybody on a task.

2026-10-21 — The office and Tasks, one press apart

  • The office’s bar has Tasks, and Tasks’ bar has Office, at the end of each, so you can go back and forth.
  • The office’s dashboard starts with Newest and Open tasks by person, then the figures, and each list in Newest is headed by a link to its own page: the grant requests, the cases, everybody’s tasks and the forums’ latest.

2026-10-21 — Invites, and every username claimed

  • You join the CB with an invite now. A moderator makes one at the desk in the Moderators’ room and shares its link, which opens the joining page: choose a username and a password of your own, keep the recovery code, and you are on. An invite can be for one person or many, and runs out after a day, a week, a month or never. Nothing records who joined with which invite; an invite counts its uses and that is all.
  • The starter password is retired, Ryan’s call, and it signs nobody on. This reverses what the street did since the CB opened: claiming your username was optional until today. If you are on with the starter password, the radio asks you to claim your username before anything else, and everything kept under it stays with it. A moderator’s username is claimed on the joining page with the moderators’ password, which signs nobody on either.
  • CLAIMED is gone from the channel, the forums and profiles, because every username is claimed. BASE still marks a moderator.
  • An email address for password recovery, if you want one. This reverses “no email”, Ryan’s call. Give one when you join, or under Account on the radio, and Email me a reset code at the front desk sends a code from our team’s mailbox, good for an hour. It is kept sealed and shown to nobody but you. What it keeps, and who handles it.

2026-10-21 — The office opens on its dashboard

  • The office opens on its Dashboard now, and the contacts have moved to Contacts in the bar.
  • The dashboard’s Newest has the forum topics posted to last, of the ones you may read, beside the newest grant requests, cases and tasks, each saying its category, who posted last and when.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: comments brought over from Notion

  • The comments on our tasks’ pages in Notion are on those tasks in Tasks now, each under its writer’s username at the time it was written, among the task’s other comments, and saying in Notion. Their writers can edit them here. Bringing them over told nobody.
  • Bringing comments over again changes the ones already brought rather than adding them twice, leaves alone one edited here, and leaves deleted one deleted here. A few that named members of the public were left in Notion.

2026-10-21 — The office: comments on templates and emails

  • A mailing’s templates and the emails filed from the team mailbox take comments too, at the foot of their pages in the office, sealed like every other comment there. They tell people as the office’s other comments do, saying only that they are on a template or an email.
  • Every template has a page of its own, opened from the list: its subject and words, who added and changed it, Edit, Send it as a mailing and Delete, and its comments. Saving one goes back to it.

2026-10-21 — Tasks and the office: comments

  • Every task in Tasks has Comments, after EspoCRM’s stream posts and Notion’s comments: the conversation about it, under its description, oldest first, with a box at the foot. A comment takes Markdown and has Preview; Ctrl or Cmd and Enter posts it.
  • So does nearly everything in the office: every contact, grant request, case and mailing, at the foot of its page, encrypted where they are kept like everything else there.
  • A comment tells the people it concerns, in their Notifications in Tasks and on their devices if they switched that on: for a task, whoever it is assigned to, anybody else on it and whoever made it; in the office, whoever added the record; and anybody who commented before and anybody it @mentions. It says who commented and on what, never the words, and opens on the comment. A comment in the office never names its record there.
  • Only whoever wrote a comment can edit it, and an edit tells only somebody it newly @mentions. Its writer or an administrator can delete it. Both Feeds show each card’s latest comments and have Comment on every card, and a comment puts its card first. Privacy and the CB guide say what is kept.

2026-10-21 — Tasks and the office: a Feed, after Notion’s

  • Tasks has a Feed beside the list, the board and the timeline: every task as a card, the one something last happened to first, saying what that was, with its description to read and tick, More for the rest of it, and Mark it completed. The filters and saved filters work on it as on the others.
  • The office has one too: grant requests and cases, and contacts if you ask for them, newest first, each with its description or notes, and Move to on a grant request or a case.
  • Neither keeps anything: both are worked out from what Tasks and the office already hold, twenty cards at a time.

2026-10-21 — The CB: Read aloud

  • A message’s three dots have Read aloud, on the channel and in direct messages: that one message, read out now in the same voice as Speak new messages aloud, whether that switch is on or off. It says who sent it, whose message it replies to, its words, and what its picture or sticker is. It stops whatever the radio was saying first; while it reads, it says Stop reading, and pressing it stops.
  • It keeps Speak aloud’s rules: a voice built into your own device and never a speech service somewhere else, and the static round it follows the dial. The CB guide says so.

2026-10-21 — The CB: replies

  • Every message on the CB has a Reply, a curved arrow between React and the three dots. It opens Replying to their name and the start of their words above the message box, and what you transmit goes out as an answer, with that line above it on everybody’s radio. Press the line to go to the message it answers. Cancel reply or Escape lets it go and keeps your words. Direct messages have it too, once a conversation is accepted.
  • A reply keeps which message it answers and nothing else of it. The line is drawn from what is on the channel now, so it changes when that message is edited, and says the message has gone once it is taken off or its 48 hours are up. Copying the quoted words into the reply would have kept them after the message, and after somebody took them back.
  • When somebody replies to me is a new notification switch, off until you switch it on, and Everything takes it in. It tells only whoever sent the message answered, and never in a room that keeps to itself. The CB guide and the privacy page say so.

2026-10-21 — Notifications: follow every room

  • Notifications on the radio have a Follow every room too, under Rooms you follow: every message in every room you can go into tells your device, and with Calls on, every call starting in one, including rooms that open later and the forums’ categories you can read. Norah’s ask, after the one for Unreads this morning. It can be a lot, and the switch says so.
  • World keeps its own switch, and Everything leaves this one as you set it. Switched off, you are back to the rooms you followed one by one. A room that keeps to itself still tells only a device that followed it there by name, so its own switch stays in it. The CB guide and the privacy page say so.

2026-10-21 — The CB: follow every room at once

  • Follow every room, at the foot of Unreads on the radio, follows every room you can go into at once, instead of pressing Follow this room in each. Unreads then lists any room where something new has been said since you last read it, in the street’s walking order, including rooms that open later and the forums’ categories you can read. Norah’s ask.
  • Switched off, Unreads goes back to the rooms you followed one by one, which it keeps as they were. It is kept in your browser only, like the rooms you follow, and a room that keeps to itself is never on it. The CB guide has it. It is for Unreads; notifications still follow the rooms you pick under Notifications.

2026-10-21 — Tasks, the office and the forums: back one step, to where you were

  • Going back in Tasks, the office and the forums goes back one step now, to the view you came from, at the height you left it, with the keyboard on the link you followed, once the view has drawn. Norah’s ask, and Ryan’s for the scroll. Your browser’s Back and Forward do it, and so does a link above every view you reached from another one, which says where it goes: ← Back to the board, say, or back to a task by its name. A view opened on its own keeps its old way up, like ← All contacts.
  • Contacts you had shown with Show more, and older topics you had opened in the forums, are shown again when you go back to them, so the place is still there. Drawing the same view again, as Forget what I have read does, keeps your place too, where it used to jump to the top.
  • Saving, cancelling, sending or deleting takes the place of the step it was done on rather than adding one after it, so Back never lands on a form you finished or something that has been deleted.
  • The tab’s own history keeps how far down each view was, beside its address, so a reload or coming back from another page finds your place as well; never a name or a word of what is in them, which the privacy page says for each of the three. The CB guide has it.

2026-10-21 — The forums: every category has its own channel on the CB

  • Every category in the forums is a room on the CB now, instead of the whole of the forums sharing one channel. Open a category, or a topic in it, and the radio’s This room retunes to that category’s own room, The forums: Coping, say, with its own channel, Be seen here, call and Follow this room. Move to another category and it follows, without the page loading again. The forums’ home, Latest, Mentions and Search are still The forums’ own room. Ryan’s ask and his calls.
  • A category’s room is for exactly the people who can read the category: a claimed username, with the category’s own roles, checked by our server for listening, sending, hosting a film, being seen, the call and notifications. So the channels of Moderators, Team Docs, Directors, Board, Back Office Operations, Grantmaking and Finance are their staff’s alone, as their topics are. A category inside another shares its room, so all of Team Docs is one.
  • Category rooms are kept off the street’s public list of rooms. The radio asks which ones you can read, so #tags, the teleporter, Who’s around and Unreads only ever name a category to people who can read it. In the World room, The forums’ line takes in what is happening in the categories you can read. The privacy page and the CB guide say how it works.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: brought over from Notion

  • The tasks made in our Notion since they were last brought into EspoCRM are in Tasks now, with their status, days, tags, priority and who is on them. Each says it came from Notion and links its page there. Notion does not say who made a task or when it was finished, so those are left blank rather than guessed.
  • Bringing tasks from Notion again updates the ones already brought rather than adding them twice, and one deleted here stays deleted, as with EspoCRM’s. Copies Notion’s recurring templates made of a task Tasks already makes were left out.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: notifications on your phone or computer

  • Something new in my Notifications in Tasks is a new switch under Notifications on the radio, for our directors and administrators, off until you switch it on. With it on, a device is told whenever something arrives in your Notifications in Tasks, with who did it and the task’s name, and pressing it opens the task. Your own changes never are. The privacy page says what a notification carries.

2026-10-21 — Four more paintings in the yurt, three more spines, Feldman and Fisher in the Cinema

  • Four more paintings hang on the yurt’s gallery wall, at Helen’s ask: Burne-Jones’s The Beguiling of Merlin, Waterhouse’s The Magic Circle and Hylas and the Nymphs, and Edward Robert Hughes’s Night with her Train of Stars, each out of copyright everywhere and each scan carrying a public domain template on its own Commons page. The Golden Stairs was the first choice and was left for Merlin, because neither scan of it carries that template.
  • Agnes Martin, Hiroshi Sugimoto and James Turrell are spines on the Art shelf in the Reading Room, each a door to their page on Wikipedia, because their work is still their own.
  • The Cinema’s Art shelf has Morton Feldman’s Rothko Chapel, the music he wrote for the chapel, played by Les Cris de Paris and the Ensemble intercontemporain on their own channel, beside the chapel’s own film; and its Philosophy shelf has more Mark Fisher: his Demonstration of Capitalist Realism from Artists Space, Kodwo Eshun’s memorial lecture from Goldsmiths, Acid Horizon on his final lectures, and two films from Epoch Philosophy.

2026-10-21 — #3ecologies in the Music Room, sparkling skies behind the portals, and one reel off the Cosmos shelf

  • The Music Room links to #3ecologies on YouTube Music, at Helen’s ask, under the record case. It is a search there, so it is a link that opens on YouTube Music and nothing plays here.
  • At MAX GLITTER the stars behind the yurt’s portal doors sparkle once the doors are open, at her ask: every star twinkling at its own pace and four-pointed glints flaring and fading across each sky. At Gentle and Regular the skies stay still, and nothing flashes.
  • Helen’s ballroom photograph hangs above the Music Room’s doors in the yurt now, at her ask, with its caption about the library turning into a masquerade ballroom.
  • The Cinema’s Cosmos shelf lost one reel, at her ask: a fan’s upload of the first episode of Carl Sagan’s Cosmos, which was not its rights holder’s.

2026-10-21 — The yurt’s doors open onto space, and the Cinema has a book

  • The doors to the Music Room, the Reading Room and the Cinema are portals now, at Helen’s ask: the Glimmer Window’s oak doors in their stone arch, the same size, and behind each one deep space, a sky of its own for each room (a violet nebula and a ringed planet, a moonlit constellation, a spiral galaxy). Open them and step through. The words under every door go through too, without opening anything.
  • The books in the yurt are smaller when shut, so the portals are the big things on the page; each opens out to its full size to be read.
  • The Cinema has a book in the yurt, bound like the songbooks: every reel on its rails, shelf by shelf, each line a link to that reel. Nothing in it plays.
  • Two more shelves in the Cinema: Films I Love, official trailers for films that are hers to love and yours to buy, rent or find elsewhere, and Things That Make Me Smile: Curb Your Enthusiasm from HBO, Peep Show, Frasier from Paramount+, Gilmore Girls and Jeff Arcuri, every one from its own official channel. Fan uploads were swapped for the official ones, and one trailer with no official upload anywhere was left off.

2026-10-21 — The Cinema, behind the yurt, with a reel for every film

  • A cinema door in the Faery Yurt opens the Cinema, at Helen’s ask: an old picture-house arch in the yurt’s timber with its screen lit and a film strip winding across the front of it, under a new heading of its own, The Cinema.
  • Every film she keeps is an old-fashioned reel on a rail on the wall, in rows rather than a bookcase, on shelves under her own headings: Philosophy (lots of Deleuze and Guattari, Bergson and Merleau-Ponty (Absurd Being’s podcasts on both, and lectures), Mark Fisher’s The Slow Cancellation of the Future, every talk of Robert Chapman’s that could be found, Astrida Neimanis on bodies of water, and Philosophy Tube), Nature, Cosmos, Weird stuff (ContraPoints), Art (Rothko and Goldsworthy, from Tate, MoMA, the Rothko Chapel and the National Galleries of Scotland), Ma, Minimalism & the Void, Poetry (Sylvia Plath and T. S. Eliot reading their own) and Relaxation. A reel can be one film, a whole channel or playlist that plays from the top with the rest to scroll through in the player, or a live camera: the Monterey Bay Aquarium’s jellies and the Okaukuejo waterhole in Etosha. A film says how long it runs before the press, a channel or playlist that it runs until you stop it, a live camera that it runs until you close it, and nothing plays until then. Every reel was pressed on the screen and played before it went up: where one of National Geographic’s playlists would not play in the United Kingdom, the reel holds its whole channel instead.
  • The Reading Room’s card catalogue has The Deleuze Seminars, his lectures online at Purdue University, on the Gilles Deleuze card, at her ask the same morning.
  • It is the Reading Room’s house two doors along, like the Music Room: claret walls, velvet curtains tied back from a silver screen, a candle in a sconce on each wall, three rows of velvet seats, and the light coming over your head from the projection box with the house lights down. The projector never flickers; its two reels turn at MAX and nowhere else. Join the Faeries and Grow a garden are in there too.
  • A job marker of its own, a ticket stub dropped under a seat, on the guild’s board; a card, a place on the map and a fractal window, like every room.

2026-10-21 — Tasks: activity and notifications

  • Every task in Tasks shows its Activity: each change made to it, newest first, who made it and what it changed from and to. A description’s change says only that it changed.
  • Notifications, beside the other views, list the changes to the tasks you are assigned to, are on or made, being put on or taken off one, a task a recurring task made for you, and @mentions of you in a description, with how many are unread in the bar and your tasks due today, tomorrow or overdue at the top. Your own changes are never there, and nobody else sees your list. The privacy page says what is kept.

2026-10-21 — The office: Givebutter on the dashboard

  • The dashboard in the office reads our Givebutter account late each evening, and when asked, and never writes to it. It shows what was given in the last 30 days against the 30 before, the recurring plans and what they give a month, every month of the last year, each campaign and what it has raised, and the newest gifts.
  • Nobody who gave is named: the office keeps no donor’s name, email, phone or address, as the privacy page says.
  • The QuickBooks summary now names only the kinds of transaction there were, so a month of deposits is no longer called deposits, payments and sales receipts.

2026-10-21 — The office: each night, the CB’s days, and QuickBooks

  • The dashboard in the office keeps a line each night of how many grant requests, cases and tasks were open, and draws them as lines from the first night on.
  • It shows how many messages went out on the CB each day for the last month: a count and nothing else, with no name, no room and no words, as the privacy page says.
  • The office can be connected to our QuickBooks. It reads the books late each evening, and when asked, and never writes to them; the dashboard shows the last 30 days of money in and this year’s income and expenses by month.
  • Each pipeline is held to its own cap of 100 on the dashboard and in Reports. The first version put both pipelines’ requests against one cap.

2026-10-21 — The office: a dashboard and EspoCRM’s reports

  • The office opens a dashboard of what came in this week against the week before, open grant requests against the cap of 100, open cases and tasks, and the newest of each, with charts of EspoCRM’s four dashboard reports. Its summary is plain sentences built from the same numbers.
  • Reports has all nine of EspoCRM’s reports, from grant requests by year and grants awarded by year to the grantees of any year and cases by status and year. Every chart says its numbers and has them in a table under it.
  • Cases no longer have the Invited In and Collaborating statuses, which were being retired; no case was at either.

2026-10-20 — The office: a mailing can start from a day

  • A mailing in the office can be narrowed to the grant requests submitted since a day, because a stage like Granted holds everybody who ever reached it. This year fills in the first of January. The preview says how many earlier requests it left out, the second press says so again, and sending is refused if the day changed after the preview.

2026-10-20 — The office: mailings to a grant stage

  • A director can email everybody at one stage of a grant pipeline from a template, from the office: Email everyone at a stage on the grant requests list, or New mailing. Templates take fill-ins such as {first}, {grant} and {amount}, and refuse one the office does not know.
  • Every email is shown filled in first, with everybody left out and why, and sending asks again, naming everybody it goes to. The team mailbox sends them within ten minutes, one email to each person, and each is filed on its contact.
  • Anybody who asked not to be emailed is left out. A mailing can be stopped while emails wait. Privacy says what a mailing keeps, and that it is the one time the office hands the mailbox’s script anybody’s address.

2026-10-20 — The office: a Gmail label adds somebody new

  • Somebody who writes to the team mailbox and is not in the office yet can be added from Gmail: put the Office label on one of their emails, on the web, on a phone or with a filter, and within ten minutes they are a contact, with the whole thread on their page. For an email we started, whoever we sent it to is added.
  • Only the name and address on the email go, and only for the emails somebody chose to label; everybody else’s email still stays in Gmail. Privacy says so.

2026-10-20 — The office: email with the team mailbox

  • Email between the team mailbox and somebody in the office is filed on their page, as EspoCRM did: newest first, each saying whether it went to them or came from them, and whole when opened, with its attachments named. A script inside the team’s Gmail sends it, every ten minutes, signed with a key of its own.
  • Only email with a contact leaves Gmail. The script is given a keyed hash of each contact’s address rather than the address, and sends an email only when somebody on it matches. Attachments stay in Gmail.
  • Writing stays in Gmail. Write to them in Gmail, Open the thread in Gmail and Reply in Gmail open it there, signed in as the team mailbox. Privacy says what the office keeps of an email.

2026-10-20 — The office takes stimpunks.org’s forms, and EspoCRM goes

  • A request for a grant, a connection or a volunteer application on stimpunks.org now arrives in the office, sent by stimpunks.org’s server and signed with a key the two servers share. The person is found by their email or added, and their grant request or case is made, once, however often it is sent.
  • EspoCRM is no longer used for anything. Its tasks, contacts, grant requests and cases are all here, and the forms that fed it feed the office instead. Privacy says how an entry arrives.

2026-10-20 — The office: cases

  • Connection requests and volunteer applications are in the office, which EspoCRM called cases, each keeping its number, sealed at rest like everything there.
  • A list and a board by status, as grant requests have, opening on the open cases. A contact’s page lists their cases beside their grant requests, and starts a new one from them.

2026-10-20 — The office: grant requests

  • Our grant requests are in the office, beside the contacts they come from, moved from EspoCRM: Mutual Aid and Creator Grants, each at a stage of its pipeline, sealed at rest like everything there.
  • A list and a board, after EspoCRM’s: filter by pipeline, stage and status, each choice saying how many it would show, with what the shown requests come to; or see one pipeline’s stages as columns and move a card by dragging it or by its own Move to. A contact’s page lists their requests, and starts a new one from them.

2026-10-20 — The office: contacts

  • The office opens, for our directors and administrators: the people and organisations we are in touch with, moving here from EspoCRM after tasks, with grant requests and cases to follow. A list a hundred at a time, a search over names, emails and phone numbers, sorting, and each contact’s page with their notes in Markdown.
  • Everything in it is encrypted where it is kept, under a key only the site’s functions hold; without the key the office is shut. Nothing about a contact is ever logged, nothing records who looks, and a search is never put in the page’s address, where the browser’s history would keep it. Privacy says what it keeps.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: tag colours

  • Tags have colours, a dot beside every tag’s name in Tasks, by what the tag is about: running things blue, money orange, people aqua, gatherings yellow, words magenta, the shop green, and looking after ourselves violet.
  • The timeline’s bars wear them: each bar filled with its first tag’s colour, and striped along its foot when its tags have more than one, with a legend above saying which is which. The colours change step for light and dark radio themes so the words on every bar can be read.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: yours includes the ones you are also on

  • A task is yours when it is assigned to you or you are also on it, and Tasks open on yours now, in the list, on the board and on the timeline. Whose picks everybody’s, one person’s, or the ones assigned to nobody.
  • Who else is on a task shows everywhere: a column in the list, a line on each board card, and on the timeline a task stands in the row of everybody on it, marked ALSO ON IT where they are not the one it is assigned to.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: a timeline

  • Tasks have a timeline, beside the list and the board: a row for each of us, the days running across, and each task a bar from the day it starts to the day it is due. Show a week, 2 weeks or 4, and move along with Earlier, This week and Later. The list’s filters work on it, and a timeline can be a saved filter.
  • That finishes moving the team’s tasks off EspoCRM: the tasks, the list, the board, saved filters, recurring tasks, checklists and the timeline are all on the street now. The CB guide has how.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: checklists you tick in place

  • A checklist in a task’s description is boxes now, as it was in EspoCRM: write a list item as - [ ] or - [x], and on the task you tick it where it stands. A tick changes that box and nothing else, so two people ticking at once both keep theirs. The CB guide says how.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: the recurring ones are made here now

  • The team’s recurring tasks are made on the street from today, not in EspoCRM: EspoCRM’s twelve are switched off and ours on, each with the same next day. The first one made here is the Weekly Editorial Meeting on Thursday. Tasks › Recurring has them all.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: recurring tasks

  • Tasks can come round again, as they did in EspoCRM: weekly, every 2 weeks or monthly. On its day a recurring task makes its task by itself, starting and due as many days after that day as it says, and its name can carry the week, the year or the month.
  • Each recurring task shows its next three tasks and the tasks it has made, and a task it made says so. Switch one off and it makes nothing until it is switched on again.
  • The team’s twelve from EspoCRM are here, switched off until EspoCRM’s own are. The CB guide has how, and the privacy page what is kept. A timeline comes next.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: a board, and saved filters

  • Tasks have a board, beside the list: the same tasks in columns by status, priority or who they are assigned to, each column saying how many it holds. Drag a card to another column to change it, or use the card’s own Move to, which works from a keyboard, a screen reader and a phone. The list’s filters work on the board too.
  • Saved filters. Save a list or a board as it is, filters, order and columns, under a name, and it is there on any device you sign on with, saying how many tasks it shows now. They are yours alone. The CB guide has how, and the privacy page what is kept.
  • Recurring tasks come next, then a timeline.

2026-10-20 — Tasks: the team’s to-do list, on the street

  • Tasks are the team’s to-do list, moving here from EspoCRM. Only our directors and administrators can read them, signed on to the CB with a claimed username, and they wear your radio’s colours, like the forums.
  • The list shows every task with its status, priority, who it is assigned to, the days it starts and is due, and its tags, and says how many tasks match. Filter by words in the name, who it is assigned to, when it is due, status, priority and tags, each choice saying how many it would show, and sort by any column. The filters and the order live in the page’s address, so a bookmark or a link keeps them.
  • A task has a Markdown description, people working on it beside whoever it is assigned to, and says who made it and who changed it last. Edit it, mark it completed, or delete it.
  • Kanban and saved filters, recurring tasks, and a timeline come next, in that order. The CB guide and the privacy page say all of this.

2026-10-20 — A page in the Wiki or Team Docs can be as long as a long document

  • The page that opens a topic in the Wiki or Team Docs can be 150,000 characters now, so the team’s longest documents go up whole as they move out of Google Docs, board minutes with their transcript included. Every other post, a reply on a page included, is still up to 20,000. The CB guide says so.

2026-10-20 — Team Docs in the forums, with templates and pictures in the words

  • The team’s documents can move out of Google Docs and into the forums. A new staff category, Team Docs, is for our moderators, directors, board and administrators only, keeps everything for good, and every page in it is a living document any of them can edit, as in the Wiki. Inside it are Field Guide, Notes, Meeting Minutes and Templates.
  • A category can hold other categories now. They stand under it at the side and on its page, and its page lists every topic in it and under them. Meeting Minutes holds a category for every year from 2024, and a new one arrives by itself each January.
  • A new topic can start from a template. Pick one over the title and press Use it, and its title and words go in for you to fill. The templates are the topics in Team Docs’ Templates, so the team keeps them up to date in the forums, the way it kept them in a folder before. A pinned one there is its read-me, not a template.
  • A picture can stand in the middle of a post’s words, for a screenshot at the step it shows. Paste a screenshot straight into the box you are writing in, drop a picture on it, or press Put it in the words, and it goes in where the cursor is. Everywhere else, pictures still sit below the words. A page in the Wiki or Team Docs holds thirty pictures and files, for a how-to with a screenshot for each step; any other post, six.
  • Tables were already there, and now the forums say so: a row to a line, a | between cells, and a line of dashes under the first row. Their headings are bold now. The CB guide and the privacy page say all of this.

2026-10-20 — The street on your Home Screen and in your Dock

  • People are adding stimpunks.world to their Docks and Home Screens, so we checked it behaves there. Chrome says it can be installed from every page, and it keeps a back button in a Mac’s Dock and in Chrome’s window, because a street of pages with no way back is a maze. Installed, each room still colours the window’s bar its own way.
  • On an iPhone or iPad, notifications need iOS or iPadOS 26 or later. Older versions save the street to the Home Screen as a Safari bookmark, and a bookmark cannot notify; from 26, every site added to the Home Screen opens as a web app, and notifications work. The CB guide, the privacy page and the radio’s Notifications tray said any iPhone would do, and say iOS 26 or later now.
  • Added to a Home Screen or a Mac’s Dock, the street starts afresh, because Apple keeps an installed web app’s storage apart from Safari’s: you sign on to the CB again there, and the dial and the animals you rescued start again too. The CB guide says so. Installed from Chrome or Edge, you stay signed on.
  • On Android, a CB notification’s little picture in the status bar is the heart now, where it was a plain white square. And the manifest, which tells an installed app its name and icons, says which app it is, so an install made before today stays the same app, and a change to it reaches installed apps without waiting out a week’s cache.

2026-10-20 — Albums and playlists play on the first press

  • Every album and playlist button on the street now plays when you press it. In these rooms a whole list only got ready when pressed, and you had to press play a second time inside the player: the records and the cave’s speaker at the Solarpunk Hermitage, the YouTube deck on Club Chronic’s stage, both decks at Pink Pony Club, the chancel in The Chappell, and the screen at Black Leather Lagoon. A single video already played on its press, and so did the lists in the other rooms. Now these do too. Nothing reaches YouTube before you press, the same as before; the press that asked for the list now plays it.

2026-10-20 — Follow keeps going through an album

  • Following somebody through an album now goes on from song to song. Helen hosted the John Coltrane and Johnny Hartman album at the Solarpunk Hermitage, Ryan pressed Follow, and his playback stopped at the end of every song. Each song in an album is a video of its own, and the radio was treating each new one as a film to get ready on its own: when a moderator is hosting, that puts the song by itself on the Hermitage’s television, where it plays to its end and stops. Now a host’s radio says which album or playlist the song is in, and Follow keeps pace with the whole list: when the host goes on to the next song, yours does too, and if they skip to another song, yours goes there, to the same place in it.
  • Pressing Follow before you have started anything gets the album’s own play button ready at the host’s song and place, and nothing goes up on the television. Catch up moves an album you are already playing to the host’s song and place, and still never presses play: it plays if yours was playing and waits if it was not.
  • Two smaller faults went with it. For about half a second between songs, the player still gives the last song’s title with its clock back at 0:00, and a host’s radio that spoke then told everybody the album had gone back to the start of that song; now nothing is said until the next song has started. And Follow used to take any stop for your own pause: now only a pause does, so a song that ended before the host’s waits for them, and a next song your player would not start is started, and the radio says so if it still will not. The privacy page says what a host’s radio now sends: the album’s or playlist’s YouTube id, beside the song’s own.

2026-10-20 — Your strip in the Dress-Up Den and Fantasy Fancy Dress, and a curtain at the back

  • Your strip is in the Dopamine Dress-Up Den and Fantasy Fancy Dress now, as well as the Bodymind Booth, so you can walk into either room on your own and bring back an avatar you kept. Signed on to the CB, press Open my strip: in the Dress-Up Den a bodymind comes back in with everything it had on, and an outfit goes back on the stand, ready to change and keep; in Fantasy Fancy Dress a bodymind comes into the fitting with its wings and ears and everything else, and when you take it back through, the Dress-Up Den knows which one it is, so Keep the changes changes that one.
  • Opening your strip only asks your Profile for what you already kept, and only when you press it. Nothing in Fantasy Fancy Dress is kept, and the privacy page says what opening your strip sends.
  • The way through to Fantasy Fancy Dress is a curtain you can see. It was a link in a sentence near the top of the Dress-Up Den, easy to miss; now it is a doorway at the back of the shop, its curtain in moons and stars drawn back with gold rope, the back room dark beyond it with a light at the far end, and a button under it, Through to Fantasy Fancy Dress. Press the curtain or the button. Your bodymind still goes through with you, and so does which avatar it is.

2026-10-20 — Howling Wolf, Starward Dreaming, Leaping Dolphins and Autumn Falls

  • Howling Wolf is a new profile effect: a wolf sitting on a crag against a full moon, raising its head and howling, with stars, cloud and a far ridge of pines over your banner. When your profile opens, the night comes up over it, the moon rises, the wolf howls and two wolves on the far ridge answer. Nothing makes a sound.
  • Starward Dreaming looks up through a clearing in the trees at the stars and the Milky Way, from beside a fire you cannot see: its light is on the trunks, its flames lick up over the foot, and its embers rise and become stars. When your profile opens, the trees close round the whole of it, a shower of embers goes up and becomes stars, and a star falls. The flames sway slowly and never flicker, so nothing in it flashes.
  • Leaping Dolphins is dolphins leaping in arcs across a sea dappled by the setting sun. When your profile opens, the sunset comes up over the whole of it and a pod of dolphins leaps across, one after another.
  • Autumn Falls is a big tree in red, orange and gold, its leaves drifting down into piles, and children popping up out of them, with one in a wheelchair beside them throwing leaves up. When your profile opens, leaves come down all over it, pile up along its foot, and the children pop up out of the piles.
  • Each is drawn in colours of its own over whatever card it is on, because moonlight, firelight, a sunset and an autumn afternoon with children in it are colours a card’s own cannot hold. As with every effect, the part that crosses your words, and the opening, are drawn only at MAX GLITTER or after Let my theme move.

2026-10-19 — Sit in the cozy, in any room with one

  • Press your familiar in a room with a free cozy and Sit in the cozy is there, beside Move it, wherever the cozy is on the page. It used to show only once a cozy was already in view, so it was easy never to find. If the cozy is out of view, your familiar goes to it and the page brings it into view.

2026-10-19 — An egg chair at the Hermitage’s campfire

  • The Solarpunk Hermitage has a Chimera Cozy: an egg chair on a swivel foot, standing on the green shag in front of the campfire’s television, its pale shell lined in the cabin’s mustard and outlined like everything else on that rug. A familiar that finds it free sits in it to watch.
  • A familiar sitting in a cozy draws its light in to just behind it, so you can see what it is sitting in.
  • Sit in the cozy no longer scrolls the page back to where your familiar was just before it moves.

2026-10-19 — Familiars stop walking back in from the corner every few seconds

  • A familiar kept vanishing and walking back in from off the page to where it was sitting, every few seconds, in a cozy or not. The check it makes now and then of where the page has put it moved it to the corner to measure, and if its last move had been a walk, the browser walked it back. It reads where it is now and never moves to measure, and a check in the build refuses the old way.

2026-10-19 — Familiars find their cozies, and keep out from under the radio

  • A familiar goes to a free cozy when one comes into view, at MAX GLITTER or when you let it move by itself: after you scroll, and when the page or the radio changes. Before, it only looked for one when it arrived or now and then while wandering, so it could sit beside a free cozy and never use it.
  • It no longer sits under the radio. The radio’s box counts as somewhere it cannot rest, so a cozy the radio is covering is not free, and a familiar the radio opens over moves out from under it. Make the radio smaller and the cozy is free again.
  • Sit in the cozy, when you press your familiar, puts it in a free cozy in view at any setting, as dragging it next to one already did.
  • A familiar waits for the room to settle before it sits down, so a cozy that comes into view once the page’s typefaces are in is not missed.

2026-10-19 — Bring my familiar shows for moderators, and says where a familiar comes from

  • Bring my familiar was hidden from moderators signed on with a password of their own, because the radio did not count a moderator’s own password as a claimed username. It does now.
  • With no familiar following you, while you are seen in a room, the radio now says so under Be seen here, and where to take one from: Chimera Obscura, or your chimeras in Profile. Once one is following you, the switch is there.

2026-10-19 — Familiars in company, with sayings and cozies, and a possum and an opossum

  • Other people’s familiars, in the room with you. With a claimed username, switch on Bring my familiar under Be seen here on the radio, and the people seen in the same room as you see your familiar there, as you see theirs. Press one to see whose it is and hear one of its sayings. It is off until you switch it on, it is never on Who’s online, and the privacy page says what is kept.
  • Chimeras have sayings. Write up to five beside its name, on the bench in Chimera Obscura or in Profile under Its name and sayings. A familiar says one when you press it, and now and then in a speech bubble when it may move by itself; they show under your chimeras in your profile too.
  • Chimera Cozies. Some rooms now have a spot of their own for a familiar to sit in, each drawn in that room’s own colours: a velvet pouf in Chimera Obscura, a cushion of moss in the Garden, a knitted floor cushion in the Latibulum and a leather footstool in the Community Library. A familiar sits in a free one, and at MAX GLITTER they wander between them.
  • Through the door. At MAX GLITTER, or when you let it move by itself, your familiar walks into each room from where it was when you left the last one, and other people’s walk in from the edge of the window when they arrive and out to it when they go.
  • A possum and an opossum are on Chimera Obscura’s shelf: the brushtail, with big dark eyes and a dark bushy tail, and the Virginia opossum, with a white face, black ears and a bare pink tail.

2026-10-19 — Familiars: a chimera that follows you from room to room

  • A chimera can come with you now. Take it with you in Chimera Obscura, or Take it with me on one of your chimeras in Profile on the radio, and it becomes your familiar: it is there in every room you walk into, in a little pool of the camera obscura’s light. The idea came from our community, after the animal orbs in Castle Crashers.
  • It sits wherever there are no words. It finds a clear spot in each room, off the text, the buttons, the pictures and the players, and if the page changes under it, it moves aside. It only crosses words while it is on the move.
  • It stays where you put it unless the dial is at MAX GLITTER, or you press it and switch on Let it move by itself. Then it wanders now and then, comes along when you scroll, and comes over to wherever your pointer or your keyboard has gone. At Gentle it jumps rather than walks.
  • Press it to move it (drag it, or Move it and the arrow keys), put it back on the table in Chimera Obscura, or Send it home. It keeps you company and that is all it does: no levels, no boosts, nothing to collect, and nothing counted. It makes no sound.
  • Your browser keeps it, and nothing is sent anywhere: what it is made of, its name, whether it may move, and roughly where in the window you last put it down, never which room. The privacy page lists it as love-familiar. Nobody else sees your familiar. It stays out of Stay Frosty, where nothing moves, and the Healing Checkpoint.

2026-10-20 — Messages stay together at Full

  • At Full, on a wide window, a channel with only a few messages no longer spreads them down the whole column with their words far below their names. The log is as tall as the column, and its messages now sit together at the top of it, as they do at every other size.

2026-10-20 — The CB marks what is new to you, and Unreads lists it

  • A dot now marks what has been said since you last read it, on World, This room, Your messages and each conversation in it. That reverses the radio’s old rule of no badge and no unread count, and only as far as a mark: it is never a number, it makes no sound, and nothing moves. A screen reader hears “something new”.
  • Unreads, a new part under Who’s around, lists World, the rooms you follow and your conversations while something new is waiting in them, and takes you there. Follow this room, beside This room, puts a room on it; which rooms you follow stays in your browser.
  • It needs a claimed username. So that reading on your phone clears the dot on your laptop, the street keeps when you last read each channel and conversation, and nothing else, for 48 hours at most; the privacy page says so, and Forget me deletes it.

2026-10-20 — Paste a picture, and messages that work like the channel

  • Paste a picture into the CB with Ctrl or Cmd and V, on the channel or in a conversation, instead of finding it with Picture. It comes to the same preview, redrawn the same way before it goes. Words pasted with it are pasted as words.
  • Direct messages work like the channel. Each message has React and the three dots on its line: Copy, Edit and Take back on yours, Copy and Report on theirs. Sticker sits beside Picture and opens the same tray. Reactions say who, never how many. An @name is drawn as a name. A first message is still words only, so a picture, a sticker and a reaction wait until the conversation is accepted.

2026-10-19 — Profile opens folded up

  • Every section of Profile starts folded, so opening it shows a short list of headings: your profile card, props, avatars, chimeras, pets, badges and jobs, and battle jacket. Press a heading to open that section, and again to fold it; open as many as you like. Each is fetched from the street only when you open it, so opening Profile is quicker and asks for less. A props notification still opens Profile straight at Your props, opened.

2026-10-19 — Account: your username and Forget me, out of Profile

  • Your username and Forget me have a button of their own, Account, beside Profile under the radio’s heading You. Claiming your username, changing its password, its recovery code, deleting it, and Forget me are all there now, and Profile is what you show: your card, your sets, your props, avatars, chimeras, pets, badges and battle jacket. Account ends in Close Account, like the other long trays.
  • Everything that told you to claim your username in Profile says Account now: the Community Center’s steps for claiming, the CB guide, the privacy page, the shelters, the Bodymind Booth, the moderators’ desk, the forums and the Adventurer’s Guild’s shelter jobs.

2026-10-19 — A Close at the foot of every long tray on the radio

  • Profile, Themes, Notifications, the stickers and Your messages each end in a button that closes them: Close Profile and the rest. You no longer have to scroll back up past everything in a tray to find the button that opened it, and closing puts you back on that button, where the tray began.

2026-10-19 — Profile and Sign off under a heading of their own, and open trays that look open

  • Profile and Sign off are under a new heading on the radio, You, just above Your radio, and Profile’s tray opens under it. With Profile open it was easy to lose where the tray began, and to scroll past the top of it looking for the button to close it; the heading marks where it starts.
  • Profile, Notifications and Themes are edged in the readout’s amber while their trays are open, as Who’s online already was, so you can see which one is out.

2026-10-19 — Bigger headings on the radio, and Be seen here says whether it is on

  • The headings between the radio’s parts are bigger, Where you are, Who’s around, Channel and the rest, so they are easier to find. In a theme whose lettering runs small they grow as far as they can while the room’s name stays the largest words on the radio.
  • Be seen here says whether it is on: Be seen here: off and Be seen here: on, the way the switches under Your radio and in Notifications do, so you can tell at a glance.

2026-10-19 — @mentions, links with words, headings, a look at your picture, and the radio in a window of its own

  • Type @ in the CB’s message box and it offers people to mention, the way # offers rooms: everybody who has spoken on the channel, everybody they mentioned, and everybody on Who’s online, alphabetically. Arrows move, Enter or Tab puts the handle in, Escape leaves what you typed. A mention is drawn as the person’s name, which opens their profile, and your own name is filled in where you are mentioned. Nothing lights up or chimes when one arrives, and a device that asked to be told about mentions is told, as before. A place in a film, @34:12, is still a place in a film.
  • A link on the CB can have words of its own: [words](an address). That reverses a rule the radio has kept since links arrived, that a link’s words were always its address, and the reason for the rule still holds: a link to another site still says which site after its words, so no words can make it look like a link on the street, and words that are an address themselves, like [paypal.com](…), are not made a link at all. The CB guide and the house norms say so.
  • # A heading, with a space after the #, is a heading in a message, and ## and ### smaller ones. Without the space, #the-den is still a room.
  • A picture is shown before it goes. Pick one and the radio shows it above the message box, the moment it is chosen and then as it was redrawn, which is exactly what will be sent, with the name of the file you picked, so you can see it is the one you meant. In a conversation under Messages too.
  • Pop out, under Your radio, moves the radio into a window of its own, as big as it is and at the size it is, like a call’s Pop out, and it stays open while you go from room to room in the window you came from. It follows you: This room, Be seen here, the room’s call and the teleporter are the room that window is on, and a link on the channel opens there. An @time and Catch up move the film in that window. Every other page has a small bar where the radio was, with Show it and Bring it back, so there is only ever one radio listening. Host this film and Follow stay with the radio in the page.
  • Speak new messages aloud works again. Since yesterday it had been saying nothing: the list of messages on the channel and the radio’s note that it was speaking had the same name, and the list being there stopped the voice from ever starting.

2026-10-19 — Profile sets: save a look and wear it again with one press

  • Your profile card’s look can be saved under a name and worn again with one press, asked for by somebody in our community who keeps a few favourites to switch between by mood, by the room they are in and for what is on: Otters, Halloween, Fairy Lights. Your profile sets, in Profile under the card, keeps each one: its theme, banner, decoration, frame, effect, name face and nameplate, and the avatar you wear. Pressing a set puts it on your card at once, and the one on your card says so. Change under each set updates it to your card as it is, renames it or deletes it.
  • Your words are not in a set. Your pronouns, what you wrote about yourself, your links and your favourite room stay as they are whichever set you wear, so something you rewrote last week is never put back by a press. Nobody else sees your sets: your profile shows only what is on your card, not what sets you have or which one you are wearing, and nothing records when you made or wore one. Privacy and the CB guide say so.
  • Claiming your username no longer signs the radio off by accident. If the radio was in the middle of listening at the moment you claimed, that listen came back refused for the handle’s old pass and the radio said the password had changed. Only a refusal of the pass it holds now signs it off. And once you press I have kept it, your profile card and your sets are there in Profile at once, rather than after you open it again. Changed: cb.js, cb.css, netlify/cb/lib.mjs, netlify/cb/lib.test.mjs, netlify/functions/cb-profile.mjs, netlify/functions/cb-pets.mjs, tools/make-profile.py, privacy.html, cb-guide.html.

2026-10-19 — Pictures in messages, every notification at once, and new lines on a phone

  • A direct message can carry a picture once the conversation is accepted. Somebody in our community testing the Sandbox had a screenshot to show Ryan and no private way to send it. Picture now sits beside Send in a conversation both of you have started, and a first message is still words only, so nobody’s first contact with you can be a picture. It is the channel’s picture: redrawn in your browser first, which takes off what the camera wrote into it, with a description if you like, fetched by the two of you and nobody else, and gone when its message goes. A reported picture goes to the moderators’ desk with its message, where it waits behind Show the picture. Privacy and the CB guide say so.
  • Everything, and anything added later is a new switch at the top of the CB’s Notifications, asked for by the same person after new switches arrived off overnight. It switches on every kind of notification there is, every message on World included, and any kind added after you press it, so you do not have to come back and look. Switch any one off and the rest stay on. It leaves the words, and the rooms you follow, as you set them.
  • On a phone, Return makes a new line in the CB’s message boxes, and Transmit, Send or Save sends, because a phone’s keyboard has no Shift and Enter. The box starts four lines tall there, and its words are big enough that an iPhone no longer zooms in when you tap it. On a computer, Enter still sends and Shift and Enter still makes a new line, and Ctrl or Cmd and Enter sends anywhere. Changed: cb.js, cb.css, cb-sw.js, desk.js, love.css, netlify/cb/lib.mjs, netlify/functions/cb-dm.mjs, netlify/functions/cb-dm-reports.mjs, the new netlify/functions/cb-dm-image.mjs, tools/check-messages.py, tools/check-push.py, privacy.html, cb-guide.html, town-hall-moderators.html.

2026-10-19 — Flying Fairy, a profile effect, and a Fairy Lights theme

  • A profile effect, Flying Fairy, Ryan’s ask. The faeries of Helen Edgar’s Faery Yurt fly across your profile, from either side, darting and hovering with their wings going, no two dressed alike, each dropping petals behind it, and petals drift down across the profile now and then. A garland of fairy lights hangs over your banner with a faery sitting on it and another hovering, and there are roses, a daisy and sparkles in the corners. When the profile opens, and once a minute, a dusk comes up over the whole profile with sparkles along its top, and a big fairy rises into it: red hair under a crown of roses and two little curled horns, a cloak of moss, a posy of red and pink roses held close, her eyes shut. She opens her butterfly wings and beats them slowly while petals and leaves fall round her and three of the yurt’s faeries fly out from her, and after about nine seconds she fades. She is our own drawing, after the look of Helen’s own profile.
  • A Fairy Lights radio theme, grown from the Fairy lights frame for profiles: its strings of bulbs, swagged across a plum night in gold, rose, mint, sky, lilac and peach, with stars between them, along the radio’s trims, dividers and sides. The radio’s name is in a soft script, and every word clears 4.5 to 1. It is a banner and a nameplate for profiles too, drawn at whatever height it has, and the face is offered for your name on your card.
  • Like every effect, it moves only at MAX GLITTER or after Let my theme move, and the bulbs twinkle slowly; nothing flashes. The liner notes and the CB guide say so. Changed: profile.js, cb-themes.js, call.js, cb.css, love.css, netlify/cb/lib.mjs, tools/make-profile.py, tools/check-contrast.py, cb-guide.html, liner-notes.html.

2026-10-19 — Who is hosting moves to Watching together

  • Who is hosting a film is under the CB’s Watching together now, headed Hosting now, asked for by Ryan. It was in the readout under the messages, where a line saying somebody was hosting read as the newest message. Catch up and Follow moved with it, beside a host in your room.
  • The radio answers Catch up, Follow, Host this film and Screen a video under them, in Watching together, so the sentence saying what you are hosting and who can see it is there too. While the radio is Small, which shows only the channel, the answer is under the readout as before.

2026-10-19 — The CB points at the World room

  • Under the CB’s Channel, beside the line about the forums, a line now says where to find the rooms active now and the calls going: the World room, asked for by Ryan. It is left off in the World room itself.
  • Calls going now, under Who’s online, lost the sentence under its heading, which said again what the heading says. Who is in a call is still shown only in its room, to anybody seen there, and the CB guide and the privacy page still say so.

2026-10-19 — A screen in every gallery of the Jungle Room

  • The Jungle Room has a screen at the top of every gallery, asked for by Ryan: any cam can go up on its gallery’s screen with the button under its leaf, so watching one big never means scrolling back to the top of the room. Every cam still opens in its own leaf where it hangs, the same size as before.
  • A screen shows nothing of its own and loads nothing until you put a cam on it. Until then it is a low gap in the leaves; when a cam goes up it opens to the size of the picture, and the line under it says what is on and that it runs until you close it. Taking it off leaves the screen empty again.
  • Each gallery’s leaves fold away, so a gallery you are not watching is one line. The cams whose owners have switched off embedding are still doors out to YouTube, with no screen button, because a screen could not show them either.

2026-10-19 — Everybody down at the Watering Hole

  • The Watering Hole’s drawing has everybody who comes down to the water, asked for by Ryan: elephants, springbok, gemsbok, zebras, rhinos, giraffes, gnus and ostriches, the animals people have seen on the waterhole cams. An elephant drinks with her trunk while her calf waits, a giraffe splays her legs to reach the water and another browses the camel-thorn, the gnus come in single file, and a rhino brings her calf down. It is still noon, so every shadow is straight under its animal, and the springbok leaping in the foreground has left hers on the ground.
  • More of the pan, too: mud cracking round the edge, tracks going down to the water, dry grass, rings where somebody is drinking, a far tree on the plain, and a round rock in the middle of the pool, which is the real Namib waterhole’s. Nothing in the drawing moves.
  • The Okaukuejo waterhole in Etosha is on the rack, after the Namib: the floodlit one, where elephant and rhino come in after dark. It is the Jungle Room’s cam, read out of that room’s own list, so it plays here or on the screen by the water and says what it is before you press it.

2026-10-19 — The CB as an icon, and as the whole window

  • The radio has a size smaller than Small, asked for by Ryan: an icon of a CB set, the aerial, the readout and the handset on its coiled cord, drawn on the radio’s own case in whichever colours you have chosen. − from Small makes it one, and pressing the icon opens the radio at Small again. Drag it to put it somewhere else.
  • The icon is still on. It listens, so a film you are hosting or following carries on and Be seen here still holds, and Fold away is still the only off. Nothing on it changes when a message comes in, and a screen reader hears nothing new at that size; the CB guide says Small is the size for keeping an ear on the channel.
  • And a size larger than Large, for anybody who wants to see only the CB: Full fills the window, with the channel as tall as it is. On a wide window the parts stand in three columns, where you are and who is around on the left, the channel in the middle, and messages, watching together and your radio on the right, each scrolling by itself. It stays put, and a room’s call window stays on top of it.
  • Pressing the middle of Move’s six dots works now. It landed on the drawing of the dots rather than the button and did nothing, drag or press, so only the edges of the button moved the radio.

2026-10-19 — A screen in the rock at Big Steep

  • Big Steep Fermentables has a rack of somebody else’s cellars under a screen in the rock, asked for by Ryan, with seven more videos he picked: a rainy evening in an old wine cellar, a wine bar by a fire, a velvet wine cellar, an underground wine cellar, wine cellar ambient music, a fantasy tavern and a cellar soundscape. Each says how long it runs before you press it, plays where it hangs or goes up on the screen, and was checked on its own watch page first.
  • The screen has nothing of its own on it, because the cellar has no whole list to put on, and it is not lit: it is a dark pane in the racks’ steel, and the room’s light still comes only through the drinks. It is the room’s first screen, so it is where the CB puts things: somebody can host what is on theirs and others can follow, and a moderator’s Screen a video goes up on it, waiting for each follower’s own press.
  • The wine bar only plays on YouTube. Its channel has switched off showing it on other sites, so its card is a door out, dashed and with no screen button, and says why on its face.
  • Two of the new videos play on into YouTube’s own mix, as the speakeasy already did, because that is how Ryan sent them. Their labels give the first video’s runtime and say nobody here has checked what the mix plays after it. A mix now starts on the first press in its card, where before it loaded a second play button.

2026-10-19 — A Treasurer in the Town Hall

  • Mila Krambo is on the walls of the Board room and Executive Session, as our Treasurer, elected Treasurer and director at the board meeting of 29 September 2026. Both rooms list the board as our Board & Governance page does, in its order and with its titles, and her name goes to her section of our About page.

2026-10-19 — A wiki in the forums

  • The forums have a Wiki, under Information: how to do things on the street, the CB and the forums, one thing to a page, for the questions people keep asking. The first pages cover making and dressing an avatar, editing your profile, writing in Markdown, changing your password, joining a call, choosing a CB theme, adopting a pet, making a chimera, collecting patches and badges, finding who’s online and which rooms are busy, giving props, and reporting bugs and asking for features.
  • Every page is a living document. The post that opens each topic in the Wiki is a WIKI PAGE, and our moderators can edit it whoever started it, so the pages can be kept up to date as the street changes. Each says who started it and who edited it last. Replies stay their writers’ own, and only they can edit them, as everywhere else in the forums.
  • Pages in the Wiki are kept for good. Something wrong or missing on one? Reply on the page, or ask in the Help desk. Privacy and the CB guide say what changed.
  • Fixed: a forum post, a suggestion or a Brass Tacks plate with https:// in it and nothing after it, as in “an address starting with https:// becomes a link”, could not be sent: the forums said they were busy. It turned up while the Wiki’s page on Markdown was being written. Those words stay words now.

2026-10-18 — Direct messages on the CB

  • The CB has direct messages, in a new part of the radio called Messages, under the channel: one person writing to another, which people have been asking for as our community moves off Discord. Both of you need a claimed username, because an unclaimed handle is anybody’s who signs on with it.
  • Nobody new can write to you until you switch on Let people message me, and somebody’s first message waits for you to answer it, press Accept, or press Reject, which deletes it and tells nobody. They can write nothing more until you do. To write to somebody, press Message under the card in their profile, or type their username under Messages.
  • Every message goes 48 hours after it was sent, like the channel. You can edit your own messages or take them back. Block stops somebody writing to you and deletes your conversation, and they are not told. Report sends a copy of one message they sent you to the moderators’ desk, which is the only way a moderator ever sees a message.
  • They are not end-to-end encrypted, and the privacy page says exactly what that means. Nothing counts them and nothing says whether somebody has read one or is typing. Switch on When somebody messages me under Notifications to be told when one arrives.

2026-10-18 — Be told when somebody gives you props

  • Notifications on the CB have a fifth switch, When somebody gives me props. Until now, props people gave you waited in Profile until you happened to open it, because nothing on the radio lights up for them. Switch it on and the device is told when somebody gives you props, with who wrote them and, unless you switched the words off, how they start. It is off until you switch it on, like every switch there.
  • Pressing it opens Profile at Your props, on the street, with the radio switched on for that page so you can Confirm or Reject them straight away.
  • Only a claimed username has props, so only a device switched on as one is told. It never says how many are waiting, and whoever wrote the props is never told anything: not whether you were notified, nor whether you read, confirmed or rejected what they wrote. Show the words of a message is now Show the words of a message, a post or props, because it covers all three. Privacy and the CB guide say all of it.

2026-10-18 — Props on your profile

  • Every profile on the CB has a Props tab, for props: a sentence or a short paragraph thanking somebody, acknowledging them or celebrating them, usually for something they did for you or for the community. Open somebody’s profile, from their handle on the channel or their name on Who’s online, and the Props tab shows the props they have been given, newest first, each with who wrote it, and a box to give them some of your own.
  • Nothing goes up until its person says yes. Props you are given wait in Your props, in Profile on the radio, where Confirm puts one on your Props tab and Reject deletes it. Nobody is told it was rejected. What you wrote for somebody waits on their tab where only you and they can read it, with Take it back, and one of yours waits for each person at a time.
  • Your Props tab is shut until you open it, with Show my props on the CB in Profile, like your pets, avatars and chimeras. While it is shut nobody can give you props and your tab shows none; what you confirmed comes back when you open it again. Giving props and having a Props tab both need a claimed username, because a prop carries a name on somebody else’s profile.
  • You can take any prop off your tab, whoever wrote one can take it back, and a moderator can take one down. Nothing counts props, and nothing says when one was written. Forget me and Delete my account delete yours. Privacy and the CB guide say all of it, and Props & Gratitude in the forums is still there for thanks the whole community reads.

2026-10-18 — Edit your messages on the CB

  • A message you sent on the CB can be edited. Edit is under its three dots, beside Copy and Take off, and opens the words in a box where they were: Enter saves and Escape leaves it as it was. ↑ in an empty message box edits your newest message, the way chat does.
  • Only the words change. The message keeps its picture, sticker and reactions, says edited and when beside its time on every radio, and still goes 48 hours after you first sent it. The words it had before are not kept anywhere.
  • Only whoever sent a message can edit it. A moderator can take any message off the air, and edit only their own: taking words down and putting words in somebody’s mouth are not the same thing. A message marked CLAIMED edits only with that username’s own password, and one marked BASE only for the base.
  • An edit tells nobody unless it @mentions somebody it did not name before, and then only them. A message taken to the forums after it was edited says edited there too. Privacy and the CB guide say all of it.

2026-10-18 — Pictures send from an iPhone

  • A picture sent on the CB from an iPhone was refused every time, with the radio saying it still carried its camera data. It did not: the radio redraws every picture before it goes, but the way Safari and every other iPhone browser save a redrawn picture writes a small block of their own into it, saying its size and colour space, and this site refuses any picture carrying that kind of block. The radio now takes that block off too, so a picture from an iPhone, or from Safari on a Mac, goes. The same fix reaches pictures on the Brass Tacks Board and in the forums.
  • This site now also refuses a picture still carrying a Photoshop block, where a photo app can write the name of a place, and the radio takes that off as well.

2026-10-18 — The forums take pictures, files and @mentions, and six more categories

  • The forums have three new categories in Community: Discourse, for the news, ideas and arguments of the day; Pets; and Props & Gratitude, for thanking somebody out loud.
  • And three in Staff, for the directors and the board: Back Office Operations, Grantmaking and Finance. As in every staff category but the Town Hall’s Executive Session, an administrator can read them too.
  • A post can carry pictures and files. This reverses what the forums said when they opened this morning, that they take words only. A picture is redrawn in your own browser before it goes, by the same code the CB uses, which takes off what a camera wrote into it, where it was taken first of all, and you can say what is in it. A file can be a PDF, plain text, Markdown, CSV, RTF, a Word, Excel, PowerPoint or OpenDocument document, or an EPUB, up to 5 MB, and is checked against what it says it is: never a web page, a script, an SVG or a document with macros. Neither has a public address. Each is fetched with your pass by people who can read its topic, a file is only ever saved to your device, and both go when their post does.
  • @mentions. This reverses “the forums send no notifications”. Typing @ offers the people in the topic and anybody claimed on Who’s online; a mention shows as the person’s name, which opens their profile, and MENTIONS YOU marks a post that names you. Mentions, beside Latest, lists where somebody has mentioned you, worked out each time you look and kept nowhere. A mention tells a device only if When somebody @mentions me is switched on there, and only if that claimed username can read the category.
  • Search reads file names and what pictures are said to show. The privacy page, the CB guide and Architecture say all of it.

2026-10-18 — The CB keeps fifty messages, for 48 hours each

  • Every channel on the CB now keeps its latest fifty messages, and each one stays for 48 hours after it was sent, rather than ten messages gone at midnight in Colorado. People on the street live in timezones all over the world, and a channel cleared at one city’s midnight was clearing in the middle of somebody else’s afternoon, so anybody asleep through a conversation never saw it. World, every room’s channel and every place on the Slake keep the same rule.
  • Everything said before tonight’s midnight in Colorado still goes at that midnight, as it was promised when it was sent. The 48 hours start with what is sent after it.
  • A message from before today says which day beside its time on the radio, yesterday or the day’s name, in your own day, and so does a room’s last busy time in the World room. A forum topic made from messages on more than one day dates each of them.
  • The privacy page says so, and so do the CB guide, the Community Center’s house norms and the forums, which are now for what needs longer than two days.

2026-10-18 — Ten more animals on Chimera Obscura’s shelf

  • A pig, a porcupine, a fox, a cat, a dog, an elephant, a penguin, an owl, a turtle and a zebra are on the shelf at Chimera Obscura, and every part of each fits every body: wings, flippers, hooves, talons and paddling feet, a snout, a beak and a trunk with tusks, floppy ears, big fan ears and ear tufts, a porcupine’s quills, a turtle’s shell and a zebra’s mane, and stripes, a tuxedo, a facial disc and a patch over one eye.
  • Arms and feet are parts of their own now, so a bear can have a penguin’s flippers and an owl’s talons. Every chimera kept in a Profile and every link made before keeps its own arms and feet and opens just as it did.
  • A pig with a porcupine’s parts is a Porkypine, the name Randimals and Stimpunks made together, now that both animals are here.

2026-10-18 — Chimeras kept in Profile, named, shown and sent as stickers

  • Keep it in my Profile, on Chimera Obscura’s bench, signed on to the CB, keeps the chimera on the table with its name. In Profile on the radio, Your chimeras lists them, each with which animal gave which part: rename one, put it back on the table to change it, download its picture, or delete it.
  • Sticker on the radio sends one of your chimeras to the channel, with its name and its parts in words. With a claimed username, Show my chimeras on the CB puts them in a new Chimeras tab in your profile. It starts off.
  • What is kept is which animal gave each part and the name, never a picture and nothing about when, under your scrambled handle; Forget me and Delete my account delete them. The privacy page has a section on it. Chimeras are drawn as elements now, wherever they appear, and on the radio on the table’s own white.

2026-10-18 — Chimera Obscura opens on the street

  • A camera obscura between The Sandbox and Your Room, painted black inside, where a lens in the roof throws a chimera onto a round white table: one creature made out of two animals, the body from one and the head, ears, mouth, tail, back, colours and markings from either, or from any animal on the shelf. It opens on an otteraccoon, the request it began with.
  • The shelf has a bear, a mouse, a panda, an armadillo, a rabbit, a badger, an otter and a raccoon, every part drawn as code and fitted to whatever body it lands on. Name your chimera, toss for one, or copy a link that carries it after the # in the address. An animal the shelf does not have yet is asked for in the suggestion box, which now opens filled in when you arrive from the room, and gets drawn for everybody. Nothing in the room calls an AI.
  • Bearmouse, Pandillo and Bunnybadger hang on its wall: Ryan’s, Chelsea’s and Inna’s chimeras, drawn by Becky Hicks for stimpunks.org’s About page and here under CC BY-SA 4.0 with her yes. Our friends at Randimals make combined animals as plush toys; no name the room suggests is one of their characters, and the Porkypine is a name they and Stimpunks made together.

2026-10-18 — A book of the Reading Room’s records on the yurt’s Bookshelf

Each line goes to that record on the Reading Room’s shelf, and nothing in the book plays. It stands above the door through to the Reading Room. Written by make-yurt-yarn.py from data/yurt-yarn.json, like the other books; changed: faery-yurt.html, love.css (§14), tools/make-yurt-yarn.py.

2026-10-18 — The profile card editor asks before it throws changes away

  • A press outside the editor, Escape or Cancel no longer throws away changes you have not saved. If anything has changed since you opened your card or last saved it, the editor asks first: Save and close, Close without saving or Keep editing. With nothing changed, it closes as it always did. Clear my card now clears the frame and the effect too, and after saving, the keyboard goes back to Edit my profile card. Changed: cb.js, cb-guide.html.

One upload on the yarn has embedding switched off by its channel, so its sleeve is a door to YouTube and says so, rather than a frame showing a refusal.
