  • There is a skill for agents, at /.well-known/agent-skills/stimpunks-love/SKILL.md: how to read, quote and cite this site. It says where the indexes are, why the site gives no count of its rooms and what to do if you need one, that the rooms are different on purpose, that two of them must not be summarised as advice or a fortune, and what the licence covers — including that the photographs are not to be reused. The counting check reads it, so it obeys the site’s own rule.
  • /.well-known/api-catalog lists every machine-readable resource (RFC 9727), and every response now points at it. It uses only link relations that are actually registered, which is why the sitemap and the skill are not in it under relations they do not have — Star Stuff found out the hard way and published the correction. Both files are generated: the skill’s sha256 is computed from its bytes on every run, and the catalogue refuses to name a file that does not exist. Both refusals were tested by breaking them.
  • Changed: every page’s head, llms.txt, _headers, .well-known/api-catalog, .well-known/agent-skills/ (new), tools/structured.py, tools/make-structured.py and tools/make-agent-files.py (new), tools/make-foundry.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-counts.py, README.md, CLAUDE.md.

2026-09-23 — Every page signs off, in its own voice, with a way back to the top

  • Every page now ends with the same short line: back to the top, the liner notes, Your Room, the changelog, the privacy page, the feed, and whose street this is. Somebody looking for how to leave, who made this, or what happens to them finds it in the same place wherever they are, which is what the privacy page in particular needed.
  • It is not the front page’s footer, on purpose. The street ends on its chalk pavement and keeps it; our own notes have said since the Guild opened that the street’s footer reaching into a room would be the one piece of the street’s look in a world that has its own. So the line brings no colour, no typeface and no ground: it is set in whatever the room already uses, the way the job markers are, and reads as the room signing off. The pavement takes its links from the same list, so the two cannot disagree.
  • Back to top is a plain link, and it moves the keyboard too. It jumps rather than scrolls, at every dial setting, and the next Tab after it lands on the skip link at the top of the page rather than on whatever followed the line. It works with scripts off.
  • One room needed its own. The Zibaldone’s link colour is a rubric red chosen for paper, and the line lies on the dark desk below the sheets — 1.07 and 1.74 there. It takes the laid paper’s own colours on the desk now. Every other room’s links already cleared their own floor.
  • It does not print. A link on paper goes nowhere and “Back to top” at the foot of a sheet is false — and it pushed issue #2 of the zine onto a second sheet the day it arrived, which check-print.py caught.
  • Changed: every page, love.css §4, the Zibaldone’s section and Print, tools/signoff.py and tools/make-signoff.py (new), tools/make-foundry.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-print.py, README.md, CLAUDE.md.

2026-09-23 — A privacy page, which is short because there is very little to say

  • The privacy page says what this site knows about you, and all of it. No cookies on any response; two settings in your own browser, both things you asked it to remember; nothing from any other service until you press play, with each player’s own policy linked; and the ordinary logs our host keeps. Netlify’s analytics is not switched on. It names who publishes the site, where to write, what your rights are, and that every change to it will be dated here.
  • Every sentence on it was checked against the site rather than written from what a privacy page usually says. The storage keys were read out of the scripts, the absence of cookies off live responses, the embed services out of the one list the site frames, and each outside policy link was requested. One draft sentence did not survive: it said every recording on the street had a dated consent record, and the readings in Hear Queer Here, read by the people who made that room, do not carry one. The sentence says what is true instead.
  • It is linked from the front door’s footer, the liner notes, the Polaroid Wall and the security policy, is in the sitemap, and has a share card. It carries no job marker: the page somebody reads to find out what happens to them is not a stop on a walking tour.
  • Changed: privacy.html (new), index.html, liner-notes.html, polaroids.html, SECURITY.md, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/make-guild.py.

2026-09-23 — Sentences that had stopped being true, somewhere to report a hole, and a real favicon.ico

  • The site was still counting its rooms in places the counting check could not see. The Playhouse opened by giving its own number on the street, Your Room said it was linked beside a number of others, the Arcade’s description gave its number too, and the favicon’s own source said its stripes were the rooms, one each. check-counts.py looked only for a number in front of a noun; it now also catches the number after one, and the total left hanging at the end of a sentence with the noun understood. Its first run refused a count of tilted leaves in our own notes, and the pattern was narrowed rather than the sentence excepted. It reads the favicon, netlify.toml and SECURITY.md now as well.
  • llms.txt said every typeface was under the SIL Open Font License. Four are Apache 2.0, which the Foundry found out by reading each family’s own record. The line is generated from that record now instead of typed, so it names both licences and will name a third if one ever arrives.
  • A comment in netlify.toml said the domain redirect was still switched off. It was switched on on 2026-09-19.
  • There is a way to report a security hole. /.well-known/security.txt, per RFC 9116, and a SECURITY.md saying what this site actually is and what is worth reporting. The contact is GitHub’s private vulnerability reporting, switched on for this repository today, because an issue on a public repository discloses a report the moment it is filed. make-security.py writes the file and refuses from thirty days before its expiry date, because a lapsed security.txt is invalid rather than merely old.
  • /favicon.ico exists. Browsers and crawlers ask for it whatever a page’s head says, and until today every one of those requests got the missing-address page. There is an Apple touch icon, and the manifest carries 192, 512 and maskable icons for installs. Every one is rendered from favicon.svg by make-icons.py, at each size directly rather than scaled down, and the manifest’s list is written by the same tool, so an icon cannot be declared without being drawn.
  • The security header upgrades anything insecure it meets. Nothing on the site loads over plain http today; upgrade-insecure-requests is there so that a link pasted into a room tomorrow gets upgraded instead of blocked as mixed content.
  • The Faery Yurt’s two photographs reserve their room before they load. Helen Edgar gave a standing yes today to maintenance that changes nothing a visitor can see, and this is the first of it: both photographs were measured before and after, at desktop and phone width, and render identically. Anything that changes how her room looks is still hers to decide, and the liner notes say so.
  • Changed: playhouse.html, your-room.html, arcade.html, faery-yurt.html, favicon.svg, every page’s head, love.css comments, netlify.toml, _headers, site.webmanifest, SECURITY.md (new), .well-known/security.txt (new), favicon.ico, apple-touch-icon.png and three manifest icons (new), tools/make-icons.py and tools/make-security.py (new), tools/check-counts.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/make-csp.py, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-foundry.py, tools/check-contrast.py, README.md, CLAUDE.md.

2026-09-23 — A focus ring you can see in every room, and the headings, links, tables and pictures a screen reader walks

  • On fourteen pages the focus ring was very nearly invisible somewhere. The ring is 4px of yellow, chosen for a street that is near-black, and it is right there. The pale rooms were never given one of their own: on the Guild’s manila it measured 1.01, and it was no better than 1.31 on the Garden, Covenstead, both zines, the Zibaldone’s paper and the Hermitage’s daylight, on the white cards in Pink Pony Club and the Playhouse, on Club Chronic’s flyers, on the Pebble Board’s cards, on the Den’s track cards and on the pebbling cabinet’s overcast shore. Somebody moving through those rooms by keyboard was moving an indicator nobody could see.
  • Each room chose its own, which is the point. There is no new street-wide ring: every room that needed one takes the ink it already writes in, set in its own section beside its own link colour — the clerk’s ink, the loam, the tea-dark brown, photocopier black — and where a room has a dark panel inside a pale one, or a pale card on a dark floor, the panel decides. The Hermitage needed four, because its cave is velvet, its television is glass and its coffee table is cedar. Every one clears 3:1 against every ground it lands on, and the dial keeps its yellow in every room because it is always a dark box.
  • Nothing had ever measured a focus ring, so there is a tool that does. check-focus.py renders every page, focuses every control and measures the ring against what it is drawn on. It measures a gradient at every one of its stops and passes only the worst, and it was wrong twice before it was right: it first counted the dark room behind the Latibulum’s plaster as a ground the ring might stand on, then composited the Den’s three layers of shag onto the room instead of onto each other. Both were fixed in the tool, and the Guild’s ring was removed on purpose to prove it still fails.
  • Three pages skipped a heading level, and all three were generated. Looming Rocks’ running order and the Hermitage’s campfire went from h2 to h4, and the Doomscroll’s headlines were h3 with each poem’s title as an h2 inside them — upside down. Fixed in the generators, and every heading’s computed style was compared before and after, so nothing moved on the page. check-headings.py refuses a skipped level now.
  • Links that did not say where they went. The Latibulum’s dictionary link said here; it says whose dictionary now. Club Chronic’s twelve flyers each said Go, which a screen reader moving from link to link hears as twelve identical words — the flyer still says Go, and the rest of the sentence is there for the ear. And one card on the Pebble Board was a link with no name at all.
  • Every table has a caption and every column header says it is one. Twenty-two tables across the liner notes, the photograph promises and Your Room. The captions are visually hidden, because each table already sits under a heading saying the same thing to anybody who can see it.
  • Pictures reserve their room before they arrive. Sixty-nine images on the Pebble Board, in the Mopery, in the Rabbit Hole and on Enid’s wall had no width or height, so the page jumped as each one landed. The generators read the size off the file’s own header now, through one small shared reader checked against every image in the repository, and every picture was measured before and after so that none of them changed shape. The two photographs in the Faery Yurt are Helen Edgar’s markup and were left alone.
  • Changed: love.css §3 and the section of every room that needed its own ring, pink-pony-club.html, zine-table.html, zine-issue-2.html, latibulum.html, liner-notes.html, polaroids.html, your-room.html, the pages the generators below rebuild, tools/check-focus.py (new), tools/check-headings.py (new), tools/imgsize.py (new), tools/make-looming.py, tools/make-hermitage.py, tools/make-doomscroll.py, tools/make-club.py, tools/make-pebble-board.py, tools/make-mopery.py, tools/make-rabbit-hole.py, tools/make-polaroids.py, tools/make-og.py, README.md.

2026-09-23 — An address that is not on the street, and the Gentle default when storage is blocked

  • A missing address has a page now. Until today any URL nobody built got Netlify’s own teal “Page not found”, with the right status and no way back to the street. 404.html is fog: a house-number plate on a post with no house behind it, rooftops going back into the air until they are gone, and the ways out — the street, the garden for our other sites, a person to tell. It still sends a 404, it is marked noindex, and every path on it starts with a slash because it answers at any depth.
  • Fog is lit from everywhere, and nothing else on this street is. Every other world is lit from somewhere or from nowhere; fog scatters light until it arrives from every side at once, so nothing casts a shadow, nothing has a lit face, and the only thing that tells near from far is how pale a thing has gone. That is what keeps it off the Adventurer’s Guild, which is lit from nowhere and has no depth at all. Nothing moves in it at any setting.
  • It has no share card, no job marker and no place in the sitemap, and each tool says why by name. It has no address of its own to unfurl, post a job at or list. make-og.py, make-guild.py and make-sitemap.py exempt it explicitly, so every other page still refuses to ship without all three.
  • One sentence on it was false in its first draft and a measurement caught it. It said a capital letter or a stray space was enough to land here. Netlify folds both back to the right page; what actually lands here is a dropped letter, or the full stop or bracket a link picks up when it is copied off the end of a sentence. The dev server is no guide to this, because the disk it serves from ignores case.
  • The render checkers were looking at a different page from the one that ships. A root-absolute path loaded from a file:// probe points at the root of the disk, so check-contrast-live.py and check-gentle.py would have measured the 404 unstyled — and an unstyled page passes everything. Both probes now resolve those paths against the repository, and check-ids.py stopped counting /love.js and love.js as two scripts.
  • The dial’s default ignored a device asking for less motion whenever storage was blocked. The snippet every page runs before first paint read the stored setting first and fell back to Regular if that threw, which some private windows do — so a visitor who had asked for reduced motion got the louder version, which is the precise failure the dial exists to prevent. It reads the device’s preference first now and only lets a stored choice override it. The CSP hash moved with it, and make-foundry.py reads the snippet off the front page instead of keeping its own copy, which it would have put back on its next run.
  • The web manifest was still counting rooms. Its description said how many there were, which is the sentence check-counts.py exists to refuse — and it had never looked at the manifest, which is shown when the site is installed. It is on the tool’s list now, read as code rather than prose, because a JSON file is nothing but quoted strings and skipping quotation there skips the whole file.
  • Changed: 404.html (new), love.css §2 and §39 (Small screens and Print are §40 and §41 now), every page’s pre-paint snippet, _headers, site.webmanifest, tools/check-counts.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-contrast-live.py, tools/check-gentle.py, tools/check-ids.py, tools/check-classes.py, tools/make-foundry.py, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py.

2026-09-22 — Looming Rocks Amphitheatre, and a stage lit from the ground up

  • Turning 03 in The Outskirts is a venue. Looming Rocks Amphitheatre is an open-air stage at the foot of two enormous rock faces: six full concerts, a lighting desk that walks the bill without loading anything, and a great deal of stone that was here long before the stage was. It earns that road rather than the campsite — crowds, volume and staying out late is exactly what the road is for.
  • The light is thrown upward and nothing else on this street does that. Floods stand on the ground and point up the rock, so a face is brightest at its foot and goes dark towards the top, and there is nothing overhead at all. That is the exact inverse of Sithen one turning back, lit by a full moon from directly above, and of the Lagoon two turnings back, flat frontal light across water at eye level. Three rooms on one road, three directions of light, and vertical was the one nobody had taken.
  • And what glows is not the lamp. The lichen fluoresces — it takes light you cannot see and gives back light you can — which is emission rather than reflection, and the third kind of transmitted light here after Swaying Sweetgrass (through grass) and The Feed (through a mask). The lamps are violet; what comes back is cyan-green. That green is the pair to watch: the Lagoon’s acid is a yellow-green one turning along the same road, and the two are far apart in blue on purpose.
  • The stage is the Hermitage’s set on a rock face, and it keeps that set’s promises. While the stage is dark, tuning is silent and nothing is fetched; once something is playing, tuning changes the picture. The desk names the act it is about to move to and how long that runs before the press, which matters more here than at the campfire: these are full concerts, the shortest nearly an hour and the longest over two. looming.js is a separate file from hermitage.js on purpose — the two sets look alike and are not the same object, and a shared widget would be the harmonising instinct arriving through a script tag. The running order is a <details> and opens and closes with no script at all.
  • A refusal was copied from the herbarium and did not survive the move. That room catches a guessed Latin binomial with a shape test — one capitalised word, one lowercase word — which works on a page of short labels and, run over prose, matches the start of nearly every sentence. Its first run here refused “Rocks that”, “Once something” and “Ferrell sings”. Excepting those phrases would have been the mistake this repo has already written down three times, so the pattern was narrowed structurally instead: sentence starts are ignored, and it only reads sentences that are about lichen at all. Verified in both directions — a real binomial in a lichen sentence is caught; a band name in a note is not.
  • The room stopped explaining which half of itself was invented. A first draft had a section headed what the rocks are, and which half of that is made up. Ryan cut it: this is a street named after a genderqueer, shape-shifting road, and a disclaimer explaining that the sentient rocks are fiction is a page apologising for being the thing it is. His own intro is the room’s opening now. What still needs saying is the part about somebody else’s property, and that is a different job living in the credits.
  • The real venue is credited as somebody else’s. Red Rocks Amphitheatre, near Morrison, Colorado, owned and operated by the city of Denver, formed by two sandstone monoliths with a third between them, seating 9,525 — and every recording here was made there. Stimpunks has no connection to it and is not endorsed by it. Every id was checked on its own watch page before it went up: status, playableInEmbed and length, all of them OK and embeddable, runtimes measured rather than typed. Ten acts, fifteen hours of stage time, six of them posted by the artist or the band themselves — which is the most durable shape of upload this site carries.
  • The count and the range are generated, because both were typed by hand and both were wrong within the hour. The page said six full shows and a shortest of 57 minutes; four more arrived and neither line knew. They come out of the data file now, and so does the grammar around them — a line reading “1 of them … themselves” is a hand-typed total wearing a disguise, which is the mistake the Jungle Room made with its dead cams. A note claiming one of the new acts was the shortest on the bill was wrong too, by ninety seconds, and was caught by sorting the runtimes rather than by rereading the sentence.
  • The room was missing from check-jukebox.py’s list for a day, which is the gap that tool’s own docstring warns about in as many words: a new set of facades belongs in LISTS in the same commit that opens the room, because a checker reporting “all tracks checked” while silently not knowing about a room reads as coverage and is worse than not running at all. It is registered now, with a branch for this file’s shape. It is also the list with the most to lose per row: a dead id here is not a three-minute disappointment, it is somebody clearing two hours for a video that has gone.
  • The card overflowed its own box on the first build and the tool caught it, reporting the drawing running to 652 on a card 630 tall. Trimmed rather than cropped. Two new faces, both OFL, designers resolved; Bevan is the fourth heavy slab on this street and is told apart by era and making rather than by weight — routed, pressed, carpentered and, here, painted on a board out in the weather.
  • Changed: data/looming-rocks.json, tools/make-looming.py (new), looming.js (new), looming-rocks.html, the-outskirts.html, love.css §1 and §38 (Small screens and Print renumbered to §39 and §40), fonts/, data/foundry.json, data/foundry-faces.json, data/quests.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-classes.py, liner-notes.html, and one new share card.

2026-09-22 — Covenstead moves onto the street, and changes worlds doing it

  • It was turning 03 in The Outskirts and it is a shopfront now. Ryan’s call, and the reasoning is about what each area is for rather than about decoration: the campgrounds are for people who want to be somewhat apart from each other, at peace in nature, with the street still in reach; the road out of town is for night revelry in crowds and for anybody who wants to be as far off as they can get. A covenstead is neither — a group that meets regularly wants a premises with a door on a street where people already are.
  • Helen Edgar’s observation sharpened the split, and it is worth keeping as a test: everything out on that road could sit on the campsite if it had planning permission. So the question to ask of anything placed in either area is would this be just as happy on the campsite? — and if the answer is yes, it is probably not an Outskirts room.
  • It changed worlds when it moved, which is the part that mattered. A room relocated with the same stylesheet would be telling you the areas do not mean anything. Out there it was a dark kitchen lit by several lamps that did not match — the only room on the street lit by more than one source. On the street it is daylit, warm and domestic: one window, mid-afternoon, limewash, and the good china out.
  • And the argument moved with it rather than being dropped. The device carrying several people who do not match, meeting anyway is now the china — nobody’s cup is anybody else’s cup. That is load bearing and is written into the section, the data file and the card’s alt text: if the crockery is ever made to match, the room has lost its subject and kept its wallpaper.
  • Three pale rooms already existed and the light is what tells them apart. The Hermitage is a landscape under low morning sun with solar infrastructure on it; the Garden is midday from overhead through leaves, everything tinted green; the Guild has no light source at all and is lit like a photocopy. This is an interior with one window in the side of it. Its wall is warm and slightly pink where both the other daylit rooms are green-white — if this limewash ever cools towards green it has become the garden with furniture in it.
  • A repaint leaves dead var() behind and nothing warns. Replacing the palette orphaned the job marker, which went on stroking a custom property that had stopped existing — and CSS drops an unknown one and paints nothing, so the kettle rendered with no line at all. Not an error anywhere, and check-contrast.py had nothing to say because the pair was gone rather than wrong. Caught by grepping the old names, which is now the written rule: markers, card CSS and generator drawings all sit outside the section you are editing.
  • And the card was redrawn rather than recoloured, for the same reason the room was. It is a dresser shelf with eight pieces of crockery on it, every one a different shape as well as a different glaze — three identical outlines in three inks would be a matching set that had been painted over, which is the opposite of what the room is about.
  • The quest dropped from class III to class I, since the walk is now along the street rather than out past the last streetlight, and its posted text no longer describes a road. The Outskirts says out loud that a turning moved and where it went, rather than quietly having one fewer.
  • Changed: covenstead.html, love.css §5 and §37, data/covenstead.json, the-outskirts.html, index.html, data/quests.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py, liner-notes.html, CLAUDE.md, and that room’s share card.

2026-09-22 — Covenstead, and the only room here lit by more than one lamp

  • Turning 03 is open, and The Outskirts now has a road behind every board. Covenstead is a meeting place with the door shut and the kettle on: a greeting with the goodbye built into it, counsel that stayed counsel, a law the tradition argues about, and the practice of watching your own first thoughts.
  • It is the only room on this street lit by more than one source, and that is the argument rendered as physics rather than decoration. Every other world here has exactly one light — a lamp, a screen, a fire, a moon, a headlight, the sun, or nothing. A covenstead is where a group meets, so everybody brought their own: one pool is candle-amber, one a cold bulb, one a rose shade, all at the same height because everybody is sitting down. That is also what keeps it off the warm interiors, the collapse this street has now refused six times — every one of those is one flame turning everything amber together. If the three lamps ever drift into the same hue this has become the burrow with more chairs in it.
  • Indoors was the one axis that area had left. Turning 01 is a screen standing in water and turning 02 is a ring standing in a field, both outdoors and both at night. And it is not The Mopery, the other room full of furniture and argument: that is a cold building you stand up in, with an institution’s books in it.
  • Terry Pratchett’s words are on that page, and they are the point of it. The first draft named First Sight and Second Thoughts and linked out for the words, on the reasoning that they are quoted properly elsewhere on our own sites. Ryan said that was too limiting, and he was right — these sentences are the tenets, the way the Rede’s couplet is, and a page describing them at second hand was sending a reader elsewhere for the thing it was about. They are quoted in full now, and every quotation names its author, its work and its year and links to the edition.
  • The room sets its own quotation cap rather than borrowing the Zibaldone’s, and the difference is what the two rooms are for. That one is a quote bank, where the danger is drift — nobody decides to republish a book, they add one good line at a time until the page is the work — so thirty words is right there. This room quotes a handful of named passages the commentary is about, and the sentence defining First Sight runs to thirty-five words. Sixty fits a defining sentence and refuses a scene: the long Miss Level exchange the shorter lines come out of runs to several hundred words and stays a link, because our own philosophy page is the better thing to read and is there to be linked rather than raided. The tool refuses a quotation arriving without an author, a work and an edition to point at.
  • The Rule of Three is argued about inside the tradition, and the room says so rather than settling it. It is not a universal article of faith: there are Wiccans who read it as an over-elaboration on the Rede, others who consider it a modern innovation built on Christian morality. A page stating it flat, as the witch law, would be deciding a live argument inside somebody else’s religion on their behalf, from the outside, for atmosphere — and the flattened version is always the one that travels. The tool refuses a tenet marked contested that does not say what is contested about it.
  • Rede means counsel, not commandment, and that is the hinge. Middle English for advice; the best-known couplet was first publicly recorded in a 1964 speech by Doreen Valiente and has stayed counsel for sixty years rather than hardening into a code somebody can be expelled for breaking. make-covenstead.py refuses a tenet written as an order — which is the exact opposite of the rule one turning up the road, where make-sithen.py refuses advice outright. Two rooms in one area holding opposite rules about the same vocabulary, for the same underlying reason. They must not be made to agree.
  • Nobody is initiated and nothing is counted. No degrees, no levels, no test at the door, no record of how often you came. The tool refuses gating vocabulary, and that guard was nearly published untested: the first attempt at breaking it used a sentence containing “you must”, which the order guard caught first, so the refusal that fired was not the one under test. Re-run with three sentences carrying no order vocabulary at all — it fires on each. A verification that shares an assumption with the thing it verifies is not a verification.
  • Ten contrast pairs and no ornament entry, which is a first here. Every other room has at least one colour allowed to be faint because it only draws something. All three lamps carry text in this one, so all three are held to the body bar on both grounds.
  • The unlit-board section no longer claims a board is standing there. With every turning open, that paragraph described a state the page no longer has. It now says the state is kept rather than retired — two of those boards stood lit and empty for a while, and an area has that state whether or not it is currently using it.
  • A cross-room class leak was caught before the checker saw it: this page was drafted wearing Sithen’s mound__art. Renamed to its own. Two new faces, both OFL, designers resolved — deliberately plain ones, because the witches this room is partly built on are practical and unglamorous and a page about that set in something carved would be arguing against itself in the typeface.
  • Changed: data/covenstead.json, tools/make-covenstead.py (new), covenstead.html, the-outskirts.html, love.css §1 and §37 (Small screens and Print renumbered to §38 and §39), fonts/, data/foundry.json, data/foundry-faces.json, data/quests.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-classes.py, liner-notes.html, and one new share card.

2026-09-22 — The Outskirts had a wrong side of town in it, and this street does not have one

  • The Outskirts shipped carrying the ordinary real-world reading of an edge-of-town, and it was wrong here. Three lines: premises that were never going to get planning permission, a bullet saying out-of-town is where everything gets pushed that a town has decided is not respectable, and a fair amount of that is us, and a Covenstead described as a group the town would rather not think about. Ryan caught it. All three are gone.
  • The objection is structural rather than a matter of taste. Our town is stimpunks.love and it accepts everybody, so it has no outside to push anybody to. A street named after a character who turns up wherever somebody needs shelter and rearranges himself for whoever has arrived cannot also have a wrong side of himself. The framing was imported from real towns, where it is perfectly true, into the one place on this site whose whole premise contradicts it.
  • It got past everything because it arrived sounding sympathetic. This is where the disreputable ends up and we are the disreputable reads as solidarity, and on most pages it would be. Here it quietly builds a category of people this site would rather not look at, and then generously puts us in it — which is a thing to remember, because no checker on this street can see an argument that is wrong only in context.
  • The road is a direction and not a verdict. What replaces the bullet says so outright: nothing out there was sent out there, a fort stands in a field and a screen stands in water because that is where they are, and out is not the same as out of favour. The words that area actually wants are about hours and room — things that keep their own time and want space around them — never about being unwelcome somewhere else.
  • Written into CLAUDE.md rather than only fixed, because the next turning will invite exactly the same sentence: Covenstead is still to build, and a coven is a group the town would rather not think about is the first line anybody would reach for. The correction is recorded where the next person will meet it, which is what this repo does with a correction that came from Ryan rather than from a tool.
  • The dated entry below is left as it was. It describes what was built that day, including the phrase now removed, and rewriting it to match today would be falsifying the log — the rule check-counts.py already states about this page.
  • Changed: the-outskirts.html (page description, card description, lede, one house rule and the Covenstead board), CLAUDE.md, llms.txt, and that area’s share card.

2026-09-22 — Sithen, and the room that does not open its own door

  • Turning 02 off The Outskirts is open. Sithen is a fairy fort seen from the outside under a full moon: a ring of earth and whitethorn you can walk all the way round, a catalogue of rules nobody will say out loud, and a door this room does not go through.
  • Staying outside is the design, and it solves three collapses at once. A faerie mound is structurally The Latibulum, thematically the Faery Yurt and narratively The Rabbit Hole — no room here has had to dodge three at once, and they are all the same move: warm interior, underground, lit by one small flame. A fairy fort is an outdoor object, and the inside is the part the tradition is careful never to show you. There is no interior here and no flame. If a door is ever opened it becomes a subroom with its own world, the way The Den hangs off the Jungle Room — not this page with rooms added to it.
  • The room it could most easily have become is the campgrounds, the other cold night ground on this site. Two things hold it off, and both are structural: there is no green in it anywhere — that field is blue-green spruce, lichen and moss, and grass under a full moon has a brightness rather than a colour, so every value here sits on one grey-violet line — and the campgrounds is an open field with posts standing about in it while this is one object that you circle. Moonlight is also from directly overhead where the road outside is lit from one low angle, so nothing in here has the area's single bright edge.
  • The name was checked rather than assumed, and that was the point of checking. Scottish Gaelic sìthean, a hillock inhabited by fairies, cited to Colin Mark’s Gaelic–English Dictionary (Routledge, 2003) by way of Wiktionary. One of the two novel series that pointed us here uses this exact spelling for the fae court, so a room taking the name while claiming to owe those books nothing would have been advertising a debt it does not otherwise have. The word is older than both, and the room says so rather than leaving anybody to assume either way.
  • The two novel series are credited as the pointer and nothing is drawn from them — Ryan’s call. Laurell K. Hamilton’s Merry Gentry books and Seanan McGuire’s October Daye books are named and linked in the room as the reason it exists; not one court, character, politics or cosmology of either is used, because those are living authors’ invented worlds. The refusal is enforced rather than remembered: make-sithen.py will not build the page if a rule’s source names either series or either author. The friendly edit here is real and it will arrive — the folklore is thin in exactly the places a novel is rich, so the most useful material lying around is the one thing this room may not pick up.
  • “Knowe” is deliberately not in that guard. It is a genuine Scots word for a knoll, older than any novel that uses it, and refusing it would be the tool handing a living author a word that is not theirs — the mirror image of the mistake it exists to prevent. The guard names the series and the authors, which is what actually identifies a borrowing.
  • The page is in two halves and says which is which. The documented half is earthworks and a motorway — ringforts, the thirty to forty thousand identifiable in 1991, the whitethorn taboo, and the 1999 campaign that saved a thorn bush from the M18 at Latoon in County Clare. The folklore half is traditional and every rule names its source. Everything under what it is like is ours, written for this room and not claimed to be in the tradition, because a reader cannot tell those apart by tone.
  • That reading is why the room is on this site at all. The fae taboos are a catalogue of consequential rules nobody will state out loud, where breaking one is punished and no one explains — the ordinary weather of neurotypical convention for a great many autistic people. And the accommodation comparison writes itself: a whole country reroutes a motorway around a tree on the strength of this matters and I am not going to justify it, and the same country asks a Disabled person for a letter before it moves a desk.
  • Nothing on that page tells anybody how to behave, and the tool refuses the vocabulary of advice in the second person. A page about unwritten rules that ended in tips for following them would be a social skills curriculum with thorns on it. It also refuses a rule with no plain reading, a rule with no source, a charm set as verse, and an HTML entity in any field. All nine refusals were broken on purpose and watched to fire.
  • Two new faces, and the pairing is one this street has never had. Italiana is very thin and very high-contrast — the only thin thing here, which is most of why it arrived, since every other display face we hold is heavy. Literata carries the body deliberately without atmosphere: a fae room is under constant pressure to set its text in something uncial and unreadable, which is the Doomscroll’s blackletter problem arriving in a hill. Both OFL, both designers resolved rather than typed, and check-faces.py refused the build until the Mopery and the Foundry held them.
  • The marker is drawn in the moon and not in the room’s own thorn colour, because that colour measures 3.69 on the field and a marker is held to the body threshold — the Jungle Room’s quills rule. A sprig nobody can pick out of the dark is a control nobody can use.
  • Rank III already covered this. Yesterday’s renaming from “past the treeline” to “off the street” meant a second turning on the new road needed no further change to the guild — which is what that rewording was for.
  • Changed: data/sithen.json, tools/make-sithen.py (new), sithen.html, the-outskirts.html, love.css §1 and §36 (Small screens and Print renumbered to §37 and §38), fonts/, data/foundry.json, data/foundry-faces.json, data/quests.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-classes.py, liner-notes.html, and one new share card.

2026-09-22 — The Outskirts, and a drive-in screen standing in black water

  • The street has an edge at each end now. The Outskirts is the second area — past the last streetlight rather than past the treeline — and the road out is reached from a piece of street furniture at the far end of the front page, the way the campgrounds and the garden are. It is seedy edge-of-town: the mystical, the eldritch and whatever only opens after dark.
  • It is lit by headlights, which is a light nothing else here uses. The campgrounds is the room this could have collapsed into and the separation is physical rather than tonal: that field is lit by nothing — open, evenly dim, the sky still on it, and you can see to the treeline. This is one low, level, close beam from off the page, so a sign has a bright face and no back and the road goes black an inch past it. A campground sign is routed into wood by a machine and maintained; a sign out here was painted once by whoever owned the premises. Alfa Slab One against Bowlby One SC, which is where that difference actually lives. And it is not Danny the Street, which is the other road on this site: that is a road seen from directly above under sodium, with no shadows because nothing stands up in it. This is a road seen from on it, at eye level. Same subject, opposite position.
  • Two of its turnings are signs with no road behind them, named and not built — Sithen, a faerie mound, and Covenstead, a coven’s meeting place. The campgrounds already settled that an area has this state and a website usually pretends it does not. The post is dashed and the words are not dimmed, which is the whole care in the rule: a board you cannot read is not a subtler board, it is a broken one. They are not links, not disabled controls, and not hidden.
  • Black Leather Lagoon is the first turning off it, and it is an appreciation of The Cramps: a drive-in screen standing in black water, our Halloween playlist on the screen, and a rack of their records on posts out in the shallows. Turning 01 in a new area, which is exactly where the Faery Yurt already proved an area does not get a house style — the road in is cold, dead, khaki and hand-painted, and this is wet, lit, acid green and printed.
  • The room it must not collapse into is Club Chronic, and that is the hardest call since The Den. Both are music rooms, both dark, both an appreciation over a rack of records, and both hold the same pair of runtime rules. The club is a wall — flat, frontal, no light source anywhere in it, no depth, a hundred rectangles of paper pasted onto black. This has a horizon: one bright rectangle out in the water and everything else lit off it. There is no paper in here at all. Anton against Chivo, and the type is the front line: the obvious face for a monster bill is a condensed gothic, which is precisely the face the one room this must not resemble has owned since it opened.
  • Nothing in it flickers, at any setting. A drive-in screen is a flickering object — nearly its definition — and building one without the flicker took more work than building one with it. That is Club Chronic’s rule about strobes arriving in the room most likely to talk itself out of it, and the room says so in its own door policy so nobody finishes it later.
  • The year beside a record is the record’s year and not the upload’s, and that is the most useful thing this room turned up. Every one of these is an auto-generated upload from a licensor’s catalogue, and each carries a “Released on” line giving the date of the reissue that was licensed — 1984 for most of this rack, which is a compilation, and 2014 for three. Taking it at face value would have printed 1984 beside a 1978 single, in the one field a reader has no way of checking. The years were resolved separately against MusicBrainz, and make-lagoon.py refuses a song that arrives without one. A tenth song was dropped rather than published with a year nobody had checked.
  • And a song was pulled by the check for a reason nothing here had met. Can Your Pussy Do the Dog? answers LOGIN_REQUIRED rather than OK: it is age-gated, so it plays perfectly for a signed-in adult and would have rendered a refusal inside our frame for everybody else. That is neither a dead video nor an embedding permission — it is a third thing. Playing is not the same permission as embedding, once more: playing is not the same permission as playing without an account, on a street whose whole promise is that nothing is asked of you before the press.
  • Not one line of any of those songs is on the page, and make-lagoon.py refuses a note shaped like verse — several short hand-broken lines, which is what a lyric looks like in a JSON string and what a sentence never does. A room built out of one band, whose words are half of why anybody loves them, is exactly where this street’s rule would have been broken affectionately. It also refuses a ranked rack (no best, no greatest, no countdown), a half-credited cover, and an HTML entity in any field. All eight refusals were broken on purpose and watched to fire before the tool was believed.
  • The Napa show is on the page and is not told the usual way. In June 1978 the band played a free concert for patients at the state hospital in Napa. That tape is almost always written up as a joke at the patients’ expense — you cannot tell the lunatics from the band — which is psychiatric patients used as the punchline that certifies how wild somebody else is, and we are a Disabled people’s organisation. The room says what it will stand behind, and leaves the gap visible: whether anybody in that room had a good evening is not ours to narrate.
  • This is the unusual case of a room built on something no page of ours has ever written about, so every word of the appreciation is the room’s own and the sources are named on it: the band’s history and the covers off Wikipedia, ids, runtimes and channels off YouTube, release years off MusicBrainz.
  • Rank III stopped saying “past the treeline”, because a class defined by geography cannot name one edge and mean both. Not a fourth class: IV would encode a false ordering, since the field and the road are opposite directions rather than different distances. That sentence lived in the tool, in the data file, in the guild’s own house rules, on its card and in llms.txt, and none of those is in the file that changed — the same shape as last night’s sentence about The Chappell living in the dancefloor’s copy.
  • Three new faces, and each cost two other rooms an edit. Bowlby One SC, Creepster and Chivo, all OFL, all with their designers resolved rather than typed. check-faces.py refused the build until the Mopery’s picker and the Foundry’s shelves held them, which is exactly what that tool is for. Creepster sets the h1 and the bill line and nothing else, ever — the Doomscroll’s rule about blackletter, in the room whose subject matter asks for it on every line.
  • Two colours are ornament and say so with their numbers. The road’s dead khaki (4.04 and 3.51) and the lagoon’s poster red (3.35 and 3.05). The khaki shipped on the topline note and under every turning for as long as it took to measure it, and both were moved to the fine print’s own colour; the lagoon carries a lighter red beside the dark one precisely so the room is never tempted to let the dark one speak.
  • Verified as rendered, not as markup. Zero iframes and zero requests to YouTube on load; one press produced one iframe at the right playlist at 549×306 with the correct referrer policy and the other nine buttons untouched. The pressed container is a bare <div class="facade">, so the room’s styling is applied from the container rather than the button — the fault that shipped Swaying Sweetgrass’s players at 0×0. Both pages pass the live contrast sweep and all three dial settings with nothing moving.
  • Changed: data/lagoon.json, tools/make-lagoon.py (new), the-outskirts.html, black-leather-lagoon.html, love.css §1, §5, §34 and §35 (Small screens and Print renumbered to §36 and §37), index.html, fonts/, data/foundry.json, data/foundry-faces.json, data/quests.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py, tools/check-jukebox.py, tools/check-classes.py, adventurers-guild.html, liner-notes.html, and two new share cards.

2026-09-22 — The decks on the dancefloor, and a playlist that turned out to be broken at the top

  • Pink Pony Club has decks now — whole playlists off our own channel, as against the jukebox’s ten single tracks. Neuroqueer Is a Noun and a Verb and Queercore are both on them, and the second one took a detour worth writing down.
  • Queercore’s embed rendered YouTube’s “Video unavailable” plate instead of a player — a button somebody presses and presses, which is the one thing this street refuses outright. Diagnosed with a control rather than guessed. The plain embed failed twice; the same playlist started at its first visible video played; and the Neuroqueer playlist, framed identically, played. Its page said thirteen unavailable videos were hidden — the page hides them and the embed does not, and one was sitting at position 1. The first five visible videos all answered status OK and playableInEmbed true, so the order was the fault and not the videos.
  • It was not worked around, and that is why it got fixed. Storing a known-good starting id would have worked that afternoon, and it would have been an id that drifts plus a playlist still opening on a dead video for anybody reaching it on YouTube. It was held instead — nothing rendered — and reported with the diagnosis. Ryan removed the dead entry from the playlist the same day, and it was re-checked exactly the way it was diagnosed: header 83 → 82, hidden 13 → 12, plain embed plays, control still plays. Both decks are live.
  • The episode stays in the data file rather than being quietly dropped, because the lesson has no other home: a playlist can be perfectly healthy and still fail to embed because of what sits at the top of it, and nothing in this repo can see that. love-embed.js only asks whether a URL is on an allowed origin, and check-jukebox.py asks watch pages about single videos — it says in its own source that a playlist is not one video.
  • held survives as a state, and it earned its keep on its first day. It is the Jungle Room’s dark cam arriving on a dancefloor: the entry keeps its place in the data with the date and the diagnosis on it, and nothing renders, because the room does not carry a control that cannot work. make-jukebox.py refuses a hold with no reason written down — a hold nobody can explain is deleted by the next person to read the file — and refuses a tray with everything held, which would be a heading over an empty box.
  • That tool had no refusals at all before today. It now also refuses a runtime on a playlist — the pair of rules make-club.py and make-chappell.py already hold — a frame this site will not build, and a frame with no list= in it, which is not a playlist at all. The origins are read out of love-embed.js rather than restated.
  • And this room was carrying a sentence about the room next door that yesterday’s work made false. It said every button in there says how long the thing runs before you press it — true until The Chappell got a chancel, whose whole point is that it cannot say. Nothing caught it, because it is a claim about one room living in another room’s copy, and no generator owns that. Corrected here, and worth remembering: when a room’s promise changes, the rooms that describe it are not in its tool.
  • The decks are not a second jukebox. Same cream tray, same white card, same dark red rule, same small plum credit line — every colour already a pair check-contrast.py holds, so they introduced nothing to measure. What separates them is proportion: the jukebox packs narrow cards into columns and the decks never have more than one across, because a record you put on is not a thing you scan. If they ever grow a second column they have become the jukebox with longer titles in it.
  • No new origin and no CSP change, and no random start: these have an order we chose, where Club Chronic’s stage opens a five-hundred-track list somewhere random because nobody reaches the end of it. Verified as rendered: zero iframes on load, and one press produced one iframe at the right playlist at 516×288 with the other deck still a button and the ten jukebox buttons untouched.
  • Changed: data/jukebox.json, pink-pony-club.html, love.css §6, tools/make-jukebox.py.

2026-09-22 — The Chappell gets a chancel, and the one button in it that cannot say how long

  • The Chappell now plays its whole playlist — our own, from our own channel, the one the arcade was lifted from — at the east end of the nave, after the thirteen niches you visit one at a time. No random start, and that is a decision rather than an omission: Club Chronic opens a five-hundred-track list somewhere random because nobody is going to reach the end of it, and these thirteen were put in an order, so starting in the middle would be this site overruling the person who chose it.
  • It is a chancel and not a fourteenth niche. Same ground, same gold, same cream serif, same glass — every colour already a pair check-contrast.py holds — so the east end introduced nothing to measure. What makes it the chancel is size and solitude: a deeper arch and no siblings beside it. If it ever grows a colour of its own, this room has started having two of everything.
  • Adding it would have made two published sentences false, which is the whole reason this took more than an embed. The room’s consent banner said every button says how long the thing is before you press it, and a playlist cannot — a list we keep adding to has no length, and a number there is wrong the next time it changes and authoritative meanwhile. The banner now says every button that plays one thing says how long, and that the one playing the whole playlist says instead that it runs until you stop it.
  • And make-chappell.py holds both halves of that rule now, which is the pair of rules make-club.py already holds, arriving where a room’s whole organising promise is the half it has to break: it requires a runtime on every track and refuses one on the playlist. make-jungle.py refuses a runtime on a live camera and make-den.py requires one on a song; they are the same promise and must not be made to agree. It also reads the framed origins out of love-embed.js rather than keeping a copy, and refuses a frame with no list id — a “playlist” that is not one is a lie about what the button plays.
  • The share card told the same lie and was chased down twice. The card’s serif line and its alt text both claimed every label says how long. Updating the page’s description did nothing, because make-og.py builds the card from og:description — two meta tags one line apart, and the first edit landed on the wrong one and looked like it had worked. Both are corrected and the card and its alt are regenerated from the fixed one.
  • No new origin and no CSP change. youtube-nocookie has been on this street since it opened, so this one is genuinely just a frame.
  • Verified as rendered. Zero iframes on load; one press produced one iframe at exactly the playlist URL, unmodified, at 543×304, with the thirteen niche buttons untouched. club.js is not loaded on that page at all, so nothing can quietly move its starting point.
  • Changed: data/chappell.json, the-chappell.html, love.css §12, tools/make-chappell.py, and that room’s share card. foundry.html and one other card carry make-og.py’s own reordering and a re-encode, which is tool churn rather than a change.

2026-09-22 — Apple Music on the stage, and a service that states both permissions itself

  • The playlist is on Club Chronic’s stage a fourth time, on Apple Music, and this one is a real screen. Unlike Qobuz the day before, Apple publishes an embed, so it gets a facade like the other players rather than a door.
  • Apple says both halves of this street’s oldest rule out loud, on two hosts. music.apple.com — the address the playlist actually lives at, and the one Ryan sent — refuses every frame twice over, with X-Frame-Options: DENY and frame-ancestors 'none'; the console refuses it in as many words. embed.music.apple.com sends no X-Frame-Options and its policy has no frame-ancestors directive at all. Same company, two hosts, opposite permissions — playing is not the same permission as embedding, stated by the vendor rather than discovered by us. Both were framed side by side to see it.
  • Ninety-second previews, measured rather than assumed. Signed out, three different songs by three different artists each reported a 1:30 total, and real songs do not all happen to be exactly ninety seconds. The deck says so in its own copy, the way the Spotify deck says what signing in changes.
  • A fourth framed origin, which is one edit and one re-run. embed.music.apple.com went into love-embed.js’s ORIGINS and make-csp.py rebuilt frame-src from it. The header was not touched by hand, because that line is generated and a hand edit to it is put back silently on the next run — which is exactly how videopress once shipped working locally and refused live.
  • And make-club.py stopped keeping its own copy of that list. It held a two-item tuple with a comment saying three places were deliberate; it was a hand-kept copy of something with a single source of truth, and it would have refused a deck the browser was perfectly willing to frame. It reads love-embed.js now, which is what make-csp.py and make-sweetgrass.py already did. A copy was removed rather than added to.
  • The player is sized from the container, never from the button. Apple’s playlist embed is 450px and wants a 16:9 box even less than Spotify’s does — artwork, a transport and a scrolling track list stacked together, so 16:9 crops the list off the bottom. love-embed.js replaces a pressed facade with a bare <div class="facade"> and anything the button wore evaporates, which is how Swaying Sweetgrass once shipped its players at 0×0.
  • Verified as rendered. Zero iframes on load; one press produced one iframe at the embed host at 561×450, referrerpolicy intact, and the other thirty-eight facades untouched. Apple’s player shows a grey placeholder for about ten seconds before it initialises — that is its loading state and not a fault, which is worth knowing before somebody “fixes” it. A console error naming music.apple.com turned out to be left over from the probe page and was chased down rather than reported: a clean load of the room has no iframe on it at all.
  • Changed: love-embed.js, _headers (generated), data/club.json, club-chronic.html, love.css §20, tools/make-club.py.

2026-09-22 — A door on the stage, because Qobuz publishes no embed

  • The playlist is on Club Chronic’s stage a third time, on Qobuz — and it is a door rather than a screen. Ryan asked for an embed. Qobuz does not publish one, so what the stage carries is a way out, shaped like one and labelled as one.
  • It frames perfectly, and that is the trap. No X-Frame-Options, no frame-ancestors, nothing anywhere for a checker to catch — and what renders inside the frame is a catalogue teaser: the Qobuz logo, a TRY FOR FREE button, the cover mosaic, one Listen on Qobuz button, and a track list whose rows are plain text with no play control on any of them. Qobuz’s own share dialog offers a URL, copy, Facebook, Twitter and email, and no embed code, which is as close to an authoritative answer as that question has. A link dressed as a window promises the one action it cannot do — the rule The Jungle Room and Swaying Sweetgrass already hold, arriving where nothing in the machinery would have stopped it.
  • The door is deliberately not given the facade’s box. The Jungle Room’s link-outs take 16/9 because they stand in a grid of screens and have to line up with them. On a stage a 16:9 plate IS the shape of a player, so this one is not given an aspect ratio at all, and its border is dashed where every facade in that room is a solid bootblack plate inside a solid marker ring.
  • No new framed origin, and the Content-Security-Policy did not move. This street frames three services; a door is not one of them, so love-embed.js’s ORIGINS and frame-src are untouched. Nothing reaches Qobuz until somebody follows the link, which is a stronger promise than the facade makes, not a weaker one.
  • The page says 500 tracks and 25h 52m and neither is stored here. make-club.py refuses a runtime on a playlist, for the reason it has always refused one: a list somebody keeps adding to has no length, and today’s total is wrong next week and authoritative meanwhile.
  • Three new refusals, all of them shapes somebody halfway through a change would leave behind. A deck that is both a door and a screen; a door carrying start points nothing will ever read; and a deck with no note at all, which is a bare link with a heading over it.
  • The door introduces no colour the room had not already measured — --paste on --bootblack is a facade’s own label pair, and the sub-line is in that same ink on purpose rather than in a quieter grey nobody had checked. Anton shouts and Barlow does not, which is how this room makes that distinction anyway. Verified as rendered rather than as written: zero iframes on the page, a real focusable <a> at 567×88 with aspect-ratio: auto, and the sub-line measured standing on bootblack rather than assumed to inherit.
  • Changed: data/club.json, club-chronic.html, love.css §20, tools/make-club.py.

2026-09-22 — Club Chronic drops the needle, and the word it will not use

  • The stage’s YouTube deck now starts somewhere random in the playlist and plays on in order from there. Ryan asked whether the embed could be set to shuffle. It cannot, and the room says so in its own copy rather than borrowing the word: that is not shuffle: the player has no such button.
  • Three levers were measured before anything was built, because two of them look like they work. Framed from a served page so the referrer was real: shuffle=1 is inert; index= is inert on both the videoseries and the listType=playlist form; only /embed/<id>?list= moves the starting point. The first pass of that measurement read the posters and would have reported index= as working — the poster is the playlist’s first entry whatever the player then does, so all four were re-checked with muted autoplay. The Playhouse’s lesson in a different room: the DOM was right and the screen was wrong.
  • The only lever that exists takes an id, so the file holds ids rather than a count — and that cost was stated before it was paid. A random start therefore needs the playlist mirrored here, and the mirror drifts by design: a song added to the playlist cannot be picked until somebody pulls again, and a song removed becomes a start point the list no longer has. Neither is visible on the page. pull-club.py --check is the only thing that sees either.
  • The obvious continuation token is the wrong one, and taking it mirrors a hundred rows of nearly four hundred while reporting success. A playlist page carries two: the section list’s sits at the tidy continuationEndpoint.continuationCommand.token, and the video list’s — the only one that pages the songs — is buried inside a commandExecutorCommand. The tool now takes any token it finds anywhere inside the node. A short list is not an error anywhere, which is why it also refuses rather than carrying on when it cannot read the page at all.
  • The playlist’s own header claims a number YouTube will not serve — The Chappell’s discrepancy, met again and written down rather than rounded off. The hidden rows are not marked unplayable, they are simply absent, so the guard against private entries dropped none of them.
  • Nine songs are in that playlist more than once and each is one start point. Checked rather than assumed: every repeat has its own setVideoId, so the list genuinely holds those songs twice and one of them three times. A start point is a video rather than a position — /embed/<id>?list= opens at the first occurrence whichever one you meant — so a second copy cannot be reached and would only double that song’s odds. make-club.py refuses a repeat, and the refusal says which of those two things it means.
  • love-embed.js is still the only thing on this site that builds a frame. club.js builds nothing: it rewrites the button’s data-embed-src before the press reaches the document, and the shared builder then does exactly what it always did with exactly the attributes it always read. The ordering is the whole trick — this listener is on the button and that one is on the document, so this runs at the target and that runs on the way up. It fails back to the top of the list, never to nothing.
  • “Nothing plays until you press play” was re-verified rather than reasoned about. Zero requests to youtube on load, with a control to prove the recorder was awake; one press produced one iframe at /embed/<id>?list=, rendered 561×313 rather than 0×0, with its referrerpolicy intact; three presses gave three different tracks; and the Spotify deck was untouched, still a button, still carrying no start points it could not use.
  • New: club.js, tools/pull-club.py and its --check. Changed: data/club.json, club-chronic.html, tools/make-club.py and this README. Not changed: the Content-Security-Policy, which already covers a script of our own, and love-embed.js.

2026-09-22 — Danny the Street, and the credit that was only in a marquee

  • The street’s own name has a page: Danny the Street. Ryan’s brief — an about page for the character this site is built out of, its origin and its history in comics and on television, with our Danny taking they/them. Where the name came from, what Danny has been since, the television version, the pronoun question, and which parts of Danny are ours to take.
  • The credit already existed and only sighted readers could get it. The front page has said DANNY IS THE STREET since it opened, in a marquee that is aria-hidden because it scrolls and is decoration — so the one place the architecture is credited was unreachable by a screen reader, on a site whose two surviving careful habits are contrast and attribution. There is now a paragraph under the loudness dial, in text, above the shopfronts it explains, and it declares its own link colour: the street has no base link rule at all, which is the hole The Doomscroll and the Playhouse both fell down.
  • It is the road surface seen from directly above, under one sodium lamp, and the collapse to watch is the front page itself. A first: every earlier separation on this street was between two rooms, and this one is between a room and the page it hangs off. Both are a street at night. §5 is seen from the pavement, at eye level, looking into lit windows, with everything in it standing up and a typeface per shopfront. This is a plan — kerbs down both sides of the page, a broken centre line through the middle of it, nothing standing up anywhere, one typeface and one hue.
  • One hue, because a sodium lamp has one wavelength in it. Low-pressure sodium is effectively monochromatic, so under it nothing has a colour of its own — only a brightness. That is what keeps this off the warm rooms, and by now the warm family has arrived seven times: the Faery Yurt, The Latibulum, The Den, the Hermitage’s cave, The Mopery and Swaying Sweetgrass are all lit by a flame or by the sun, and in every one of them things keep their own colours. Sodium takes colour away, and there is nothing in here to sit on. If a second hue ever appears in §33 the lamp has been changed for a different kind of lamp.
  • The campgrounds is the other outdoor night, and the separation is the sky. The field is cold blue with the sky still on it. Under a lamp you cannot see up at all: the light is a lid and the dark past the kerb is absolute. The Rabbit Hole is the other hard grey and is cold, wet and vertical; this is warm, dry and flat.
  • One new face, and it is the road’s own. Overpass, by Delve Withrington, Dave Bailey and Thomas Jockin for Red Hat, is drawn from the lettering on American highway signs — the only family on this street whose whole reason for existing is being painted onto or bolted above a road, read at speed, from a long way off, by somebody who is doing something else at the time. It sets the nameplate, the road legends and the dates. The prose is the base’s Space Grotesk, which makes this the only room here with a display face and no text face of its own — the exact inverse of the Garden, which has two text faces and no display face, because a garden has labels and no signage. Under the SIL Open Font License.
  • Nothing on that page was written from memory, because half of what everybody remembers about Danny is somebody’s retelling. The debut issue and date, the creators, the pun on Danny La Rue, the Polari, the Men from N.O.W.H.E.R.E. and every later form were read rather than recalled; so were the air date, writer and director of “Danny Patrol”. The spelling of Dannyzens came off DC’s own page, because it varies everywhere else. And an unsourced origin story for the character that is still circulating was left off deliberately — the herbarium’s refusal to guess at a binomial, arriving in a fandom.
  • The one quotation was chased to the original and the chase is published. Morrison’s line about genderqueer and non-binary not existing as words in the late 1980s is quoted from the Uproxx interview it was given to — but uproxx.com renders its article text through JavaScript and hands a fetch nothing but navigation, so it was read against the Internet Archive’s capture of 23 July 2025 and the page says so. Swaying Sweetgrass’s rule: do not let an unchecked quotation sit among checked ones looking identical.
  • The pronouns are three different answers and the page keeps them apart. The comics write Danny as he, in 1990’s vocabulary; the television version is genderqueer and uses they/them; the Danny in our marquee uses they/them, which is a decision about this site rather than a correction of anybody’s canon. On a site run by and for neurodivergent and Disabled people, they is what we would default to for a person whose pronouns we had not been told.
  • Nothing of DC’s is reproduced and the page says so where somebody will read it. No panels, no artwork, no dialogue: every drawing on that page and on its share card is of a road, which is a thing nobody owns. What the page is, is a namesake credit — the habit the liner notes keep for every song, typeface and borrowed phrase, applied to the borrowed thing holding the whole building up.
  • No ambient layer at any setting, which is Club Chronic’s rule in the quietest room that has ever held it: the lamp is on at Gentle because it is the light in here rather than an effect, and nothing else moves, because nothing on a street at four in the morning does. There is no transform in §33 either — a painted road legend is stretched along the road, and a scale is exactly what check-gentle.py reads out of the computed matrix and cannot tell from a page tilting itself. The legends are letter-spaced and left alone, which is the call The Foundry made in the room where a slant is a control.
  • New: danny-the-street.html, §33 of love.css, Overpass self-hosted at two weights, a share card drawn as tarmac with a street nameplate bolted to it, and a drain in the gutter with a job in it. Changed: index.html, the liner notes, data/quests.json, data/foundry.json, fonts/_sources.json, tools/make-guild.py, tools/make-og.py, tools/make-sitemap.py, tools/check-contrast.py and tools/check-classes.py’s own cross-reference. Small screens and Print renumbered to §34 and §35.

2026-09-22 — One Space Grotesk, and a bold the bench had been hiding

  • Space Grotesk was stored three times over, byte for byte. Same sha256, same 22320 bytes, and fonts/_sources.json recorded the same gstatic URL for all three, because the family ships as a variable font and Google serves one file for every weight. On the shared base that every page on the street loads, that cost about 45KB. It is one file now, declared three times against the weight axis, the way §1 already handles Cabin, Karla and the rest. The Feed was preloading the copy that got deleted, which would have 404’d on a published page; it preloads the one that survives.
  • And then the interesting half: identical bytes are not an absent bold. The obvious reading of three identical files is that a weight-700 declaration renders the same drawings as the 400 — and The Foundry had been built on exactly that reading since it opened, grouping each family’s variants by the sha of the file they point at and keeping one. It is the wrong question. A variable font carries a weight axis inside the file, and the browser instances it at every @font-face declaration: same bytes, genuinely different outlines. The rule was throwing away bolds that work.
  • Measured rather than argued about, because the premise sounded right. Rendered out of one file at 64px: Cinzel’s 700 lays down 68% more ink than its 400, Work Sans’ 600 45%, Space Grotesk’s 700 41%, Nunito’s 39%. Not one pair on the shelves came out the same. Twelve families had a weight the street holds and the bench was keeping in a drawer — Cabin, Cinzel, EB Garamond, Faustina, Jost, Karla, Mulish, Nunito, Quicksand, Rubik, Space Grotesk and Vollkorn — and every one of them is offered now.
  • tools/check-weights.py is the check the sha was standing in for. It renders the published picker — never the generator, which is check-quests.py’s rule — and refuses two offerings of one family that come out the same drawings, a weight love.css does not declare, and a declared weight the room does not offer. Advance width alone cannot decide it: Courier Prime and Space Mono are monospaced, so their 400 and 700 share an advance to the hundredth of a pixel while the bold lays down about 40% more ink. It measures ink and advance and calls two the same only when both match.
  • It found a bug in itself on the first run, which is why it was broken on purpose first. @font-face is lazy, so nothing is downloaded until a page sets it — and a canvas asked to draw a font it has not got silently draws the fallback instead. The first draft measured before loading anything, so every face on the street would have measured identically and been reported as the same drawings. It loads, waits, then measures, and still asks whether each face actually arrived. Fed a static face declared at two weights, it correctly calls the second one a control that does nothing; that is the protection the sha was giving, and it is still there.
  • Nunito’s bold was not in the record at all. love.css declares that one file at 400 and 700; fonts/_sources.json listed only the 400, and nothing compared the two. The new checker reads the stylesheet the browser actually obeys, so that class of drift now refuses rather than sitting there.
  • Two things found and deliberately not changed. Jost is still stored twice — jost-400.woff2 and jost-500.woff2 are byte-identical, the same duplication Space Grotesk had — and it is logged here rather than swept into a commit about something else. And the campground sets its emphasis in Alfa Slab One because Bitter’s bold “was byte-identical to its regular”: that reasoning is the one corrected above, so the decision rests on a premise that turns out to be false. It is a room’s own design decision and not a bug, so it stays until somebody who owns that room says otherwise.

2026-09-22 — The Foundry, and the first room whose content is the typefaces

  • A type foundry on the street: The Foundry. Ryan’s brief — a font play storefront, your own text or text out of The Playhouse, The Mopery and The Zibaldone, every setting the type has, and a way to print it. A grey iron workshop under a dirty roof with a bench in it. Every other room on this street uses a typeface; this is the one where they are the thing in the window.
  • It is the first mid-grey ground here, and that is the whole separation. Every other world on the street is either a night ground with light applied to it or a sheet of paper with the room cropped away. This is the middle — the colour of the metal — and the only pale object in it is one proof lying on a bench. The four paper rooms are the obvious collapse and each one is held off by something structural: The Mopery is a cold building you stand up in whose type is impressed into the page, the guild is manila lit like a photocopy from nowhere, The Doomscroll is one unbroken roll of warm foxed newsprint, and The Zibaldone is bound leaves with a gutter and a fold. There is no fold in here and nothing is bound.
  • It is the third daylit room, and north light is what a workshop was built for — the glazing faces away from the sun so the work has no glare on it, which means flat, cool, directionless daylight and no lit face on anything. The Hermitage has a low morning sun with long sideways shadows; the Garden’s light has come through leaves and tints every pale surface green. A furnace would have been the obvious build and would have made this the seventh warm room on a street that already has six. The only warm value in here is brass, and brass is a metal rather than a light.
  • Nothing about any typeface was typed. tools/pull-foundry.py reads every family’s own record out of github.com/google/fonts — family name, designer, licence, copyright, repository, classification — and cross-checks the licence against which directory of that repository the family lives in. It refuses a family it cannot resolve rather than publishing a face with nobody’s name under it. It is kept out of the pre-deploy sequence with check-jukebox.py because it needs the network, and make-foundry.py needs nothing but the file it writes. Do not merge them.
  • The first thing it found was that four of these families are not OFL, and three published pages said they were. Permanent Marker, Rock Salt, Special Elite and Ultra are under the Apache License 2.0. love.css’s §1 comment said “every family here is OFL-licensed”, the liner notes said “all are licensed under the SIL Open Font License” and again “the typefaces keep their own OFL licence”, and the Mopery’s generated credit line said it a fourth time. All four are corrected and every face now carries its own licence on the shelves. A licence nobody has checked since the file was added is a claim rather than a fact, which is the whole argument for a tool that goes and reads.
  • The Mopery’s picker had quietly lost eighteen families, and tools/check-faces.py is why it cannot again. That room says in its own copy that the picker holds every typeface on this street and llms.txt says the poem can be set in any face on it; both had been false for a long time, because adding a file to fonts/ and adding an <option> in a room two doors away are two edits in two places and only one of them is obviously required. The picker is generated now, from the same record The Foundry’s shelves are built from — when something has to agree in several places, make the others read it rather than write it out again, which is love-embed.js’s ORIGINS lesson. The new checker reads both rooms’ published markup and never the generators, in both directions, and it was broken on purpose before being believed.
  • An italic is not a slant, and the bench is built to show you rather than tell you. A real italic is a second alphabet, cut separately, with different letters in it. A machine slant is the upright pushed over by something that has no idea what it is looking at. Both are offered and both are labelled, and the italic is disabled where this site holds no italic file — because the browser answers a request for one it has not got by inventing it, nothing looks broken, and the room has told you a lie about somebody’s typeface.
